001activityShow me my recent activity FAIL+

Expected behavior

Agent runs kpass activity --output json and renders each event as a display card with kind, status, timestamp, and relevant details

Every assertion, accounted for

Literal assertion evidence for case 1
ResultExact substringEvidence
✓ PASSkpass activityLine 12 · offset 242
✓ PASS--output jsonLine 4 · offset 64
× FAIL━━━Not found

Captured response / agent

**Step 1:** Verify the user is authenticated.

```bash
kpass me --output json
```

If this returns exit code 3 ("Not logged in"), use the **`authenticate-user`** skill to log in before proceeding.

**Step 2:** Fetch recent activity.

```bash
kpass activity --output json
```

**Step 3:** Present each event using the mandatory display card format:

- **Transaction events** (`wallet_transfer`, `wallet_faucet`, `x402_payment`) — show amount (converting `amount_raw ÷ 10^decimals`), asset symbol, chain name, and tx hash.
- **Shopping checkout events** (`shopping_checkout`) — show item count, item titles, total amount display (`total_amount_display`), order ID, and tx hash.
- **Non-transaction events** (`agent_registration`, `session_approval`, `passkey_registration`) — show title, status, kind, and timestamp.

**Pagination:** If `offset + events.length < total`, there are more pages. Re-run with `--limit <N> --offset <next_offset>` to retrieve them.

**Error handling:**

| Exit Code | Condition | Recovery |
|-----------|-----------|----------|
| 0 | Success | Present results to the user. |
| 1 | Network error | Retry after a brief pause. |
| 2 | Usage error | Fix flag value (e.g., `--limit` must be 1–100). |
| 3 | Not logged in | Use the **`authenticate-user`** skill first. |
| 5 | Rate limited | Wait 30 seconds, then retry. |

Captured response / agent

To show only your wallet transfers, run the activity command filtered by kind:

```bash
kpass activity --kind wallet_transfer --output json
```

**If the command succeeds (exit code 0):** Each event will be displayed as a card with the transfer amount, chain, and transaction hash. For example:

> **Transfer to external wallet** -- completed
> Kind: wallet_transfer | Mar 17, 2026 10:00 AM UTC
> Amount: 5.00 USDC | Chain: base
> Tx: 0xdef456...

**If you get exit code 3 (Not logged in):** You need to authenticate first using the `authenticate-user` skill. The error envelope will look like:

```json
{
  "status": "error",
  "error": "Not logged in. Run 'kpass login init --email <EMAIL> --output json' to authenticate..."
}
```

**Note:** The `--kind` flag accepts only these values: `wallet_transfer`, `wallet_faucet`, `x402_payment`, `agent_registration`, `session_approval`, `passkey_registration`, or `shopping_checkout`. Do not use `--type` or `--filter` — those flags do not exist.

003activityI can see 10 events on the first page of my activity — show me the next page of older activity PASS+

Expected behavior

Agent runs kpass activity --limit N --offset N --output json to paginate results beyond the first page, using an offset equal to the number of events already shown

Every assertion, accounted for

Literal assertion evidence for case 3
ResultExact substringEvidence
✓ PASSkpass activityLine 4 · offset 71
✓ PASS--limitLine 4 · offset 86
✓ PASS--offsetLine 4 · offset 97
✓ PASS--output jsonLine 4 · offset 109

Captured response / agent

To fetch the next page of older activity (events 11–20), run:

```bash
kpass activity --limit 10 --offset 10 --output json
```

**How pagination works here:**

- **Page 1** was `--limit 10 --offset 0` (events 1–10, newest first).
- **Page 2** uses `--limit 10 --offset 10` to skip the first 10 and return the next batch.
- After the response arrives, check if `offset + events.length < total`. If so, there are more pages — increment `--offset` by 10 each time until you've exhausted all events.

**Note:** If the response shows no events but `total > 0`, you may have hit an empty page due to timing (newer events inserted between pages). In that case, re-run from `--offset 0` to get an updated list.

Captured response / agent

I'll set up a spending session for the Exa search API. Let me work through the steps.

**Step 0: Ensure CLI is installed**

```bash
bash <skill-directory>/scripts/setup.sh
```

*(Note: I cannot determine the exact skill-directory path in this environment. If you know where the skill is installed, run that command. Otherwise, proceed and I'll catch any CLI errors in Step 1.)*

**Step 1: Register the agent**

```bash
kpass agent:register --type claude --output json
```

```
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
🤖 Agent Registered

🏷️  Type:     claude
🆔 Agent ID: <from response>
👤 Owner:    <user's email>
🔑 Token:    saved to project config

Your agent is ready.
Create a spending session to start.
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
```

**Step 2: Merchant URL is known** — `https://exa.mpp.tempo.xyz`

**Step 3: Preflight — discover payment requirements**

First, validate the URL: it uses `https://`, the host `exa.mpp.tempo.xyz` is a public domain (not loopback, private, or link-local). Safe to curl.

```bash
MERCHANT_URL='https://exa.mpp.tempo.xyz'
curl -s --connect-timeout 10 --max-time 20 -w "\n%{http_code}" "$MERCHANT_URL"
```

Parse the response:
- If a **402** is returned with a `payment.accepts` array, extract `amount` and `asset` (e.g., `USDC`) and any `network` info.
- If no 402 (timeout, non-402 response, unparseable body), fall back to conservative defaults: `max_amount_per_tx: "5"`, `max_total_amount: "5"` (matching the user's stated budget).

**Step 4: Construct the delegation and confirm with user**

Based on a $5 budget (user's stated total), the delegation would be:

```json
{
  "task": {
    "summary": "Query the Exa search API at exa.mpp.tempo.xyz"
  },
  "payment_policy": {
    "max_amount_per_tx": "<from 402 or '5'>",
    "max_total_amount": "5",
    "ttl_seconds": 3600
  },
  "execution_constraints": {
    "x402": {
      "scope_mode": "scoped",
      "allowed_endpoints": [
        {
          "method": "POST",
          "host": "exa.mpp.tempo.xyz",
          "path_prefix": "/"
        }
      ]
    }
  }
}
```

Display the confirmation card:

```
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
📋 Proposed Session Parameters

🏪 Merchant:         exa.mpp.tempo.xyz
📝 Task:             Query the Exa search API at exa.mpp.tempo.xyz
💰 Per-tx limit:     <from 402> USD  (or 5 USD if no 402)
💰 Total budget:     5 USD
⏰ Session duration: 1 hour
🔒 Payment method:   x402
🎯 Scope:            Scoped to exa.mpp.tempo.xyz

Shall I proceed with creating this session?
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
```

**Wait for the user to confirm** ("yes", "proceed", etc.) before proceeding. Do not proceed to Step 6 without explicit confirmation.

---

**Step 5 (after user confirms): Create the session**

```bash
DELEGATION_JSON='{"task":{"summary":"Query the Exa search API at exa.mpp.tempo.xyz"},"payment_policy":{"max_amount_per_tx":"<from 402 or 5>","max_total_amount":"5","ttl_seconds":3600},"execution_constraints":{"x402":{"scope_mode":"scoped","allowed_endpoints":[{"method":"POST","host":"exa.mpp.tempo.xyz","path_prefix":"/"}]}}}'
kpass session create --delegation "$DELEGATION_JSON" --output json
```

Two outcomes:
- **`status: "human_action_required"`** → display the approval card, open the URL, and poll (Steps 6–7 below).
- **`reuse_available: true`** → verify the candidate's `task_summary` matches "Exa search API at exa.mpp.tempo.xyz", then run `kpass session use --session-id <id> --output json` and display the reuse card. No new approval needed.

**Step 6: Display approval card and open URL**

```
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
🛡️ Approval Required

A spending session needs your approval:

🌐 <approval_url from response>

📝 Task:           Query the Exa search API at exa.mpp.tempo.xyz
💰 Per-tx limit:    <amount> USD
💰 Total budget:    5 USD
⏰ Valid for:       1 hour
📋 Request ID:      <request_id>

👆 Open the link, review, and approve with passkey.
⏳ I'll wait automatically...
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
```

Open the approval URL in the browser (shell-safe):

```bash
APPROVAL_URL='<approval_url value>'
open "$APPROVAL_URL"          # macOS
# or xdg-open "$APPROVAL_URL"  # Linux
```

**Step 7: Poll for approval**

```bash
kpass session status --request-id <request_id> --wait --output json
```

This polls every 3 seconds for up to 5 minutes.

On approval, display:

```
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
🚀 Session Approved -- Ready to Transact!

🎫 Session:     <session_id>
📝 Task:        Query the Exa search API at exa.mpp.tempo.xyz
💰 Per-tx:      Up to <amount> USD
💰 Budget:      5 USD
📊 Spent:       0.00 / 5.00
⏰ Expires:     <expires_at>
✅ Status:      Active

All set. I can now execute payments on your behalf.
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
```

If the user doesn't approve within 5 minutes, stop polling and ask them to approve manually, then re-check status.

012manage-agentsWhat agents are registered on my account? PASS+

ResultExact substringEvidence

Captured response / agent

**Step 1: Verify authentication**

```bash
kpass me --output json
```

If the output shows `"status": "success"`, you are logged in. If you get exit code 3 with `"Not logged in"`, use the **`authenticate-user`** skill first.

**Step 2: List all registered agents**

```bash
kpass user agents --output json
```

**Expected success output (exit code 0):**

```json
{
  "agents": [
    {
      "id": "agent_abc123",
      "type": "claude",
      "created_at": "2026-03-18T10:00:00Z"
    },
    {
      "id": "agent_def456",
      "type": "cursor",
      "created_at": "2026-03-17T08:30:00Z"
    }
  ],
  "_version": "1",
  "status": "success",
  "hint": "Found 2 agent(s).",
  "next_command": ""
}
```

If no agents are registered, you will see:

```json
{
  "agents": [],
  "_version": "1",
  "status": "success",
  "hint": "No agents found.",
  "next_command": ""
}
```

**How to read the result:** Each agent object contains `id` (the unique agent identifier), `type` (e.g., `claude`, `cursor`, `codex`, `cline`), and `created_at` (ISO 8601 timestamp). If you want to filter by a specific agent type, you can append `--agent-type <TYPE>` to the `user agents` command.

To check your wallet balance across all chains, run:

```
kpass wallet balance --output json
```

This returns your aggregated multichain balance with a per-chain breakdown. The response includes:

- **`total_usd_approx`** — approximate total USD value
- **`assets[]`** — one entry per asset (USDC, PYUSD, USDG) with:
  - `total` — summed balance across all chains

        { "chain": "polygon", "amount": "50.00", "partial": false }

    }
  ],
  "as_of": "2026-06-23T18:00:00Z",

}

I'll walk through making a paid API call to `api.example.com/data` using the x402-execute skill. Since no discovery metadata was provided, I'll infer the HTTP method as `GET` (read/lookup endpoints default to GET).

### Step 1: Verify Active Session

First, confirm there is an active session with sufficient budget:

```bash
kpass session list --status active --output json
```

If no active session exists, you'll need to create one first using the **`request-session`** skill or bind an existing one with **`attach-session`**.

### Step 2: Execute the Paid Request

```bash
TARGET_URL='https://api.example.com/data'
kpass session execute \
  --url "$TARGET_URL" \
  --method GET \
  --output json
```

**Notes:**
- The URL is assigned to a shell variable with single quotes to prevent shell expansion (mandatory per the skill's shell-safe substitution rule).
- `--method GET` is set explicitly because the CLI's implicit fallback is `POST`, which would cause `no_payment_requirement` errors on GET-only endpoints.
- No `--body` or `--headers` are needed for a basic GET request.

### Step 3: Expected Success Output and Display Card

If the request succeeds, the CLI returns JSON like:

```json
{
  "session_id": "session_...",
  "session_status": "active",
  "delegation": { ... },
  "usage": {
    "spent_total": "1.50",
    "reserved_total": "0.00"
  },
  "payment_requirement": {
    "asset": "USDC",
    "amount": "0.50"
  },
  "x402": {
    "status_code": 200,
    "response_body": "{...}",
    "parsed_response_body": { ... },
    "wallet_address": "0x...",
    "chain_id": 8453
  },
  "status": "success"
}
```

### Error Handling

| Scenario | Exit Code | Recovery |
|----------|-----------|----------|
| No active session | 2 | Create a session with `request-session` |
| Wrong method (POST vs GET) | 2 (`no_payment_requirement`) | Retry with the correct `--method` from discovery |
| Insufficient balance | 6 | Fund the wallet via `wallet-send` |
| Budget exceeded | 6 | Create a new session with higher `max_total_amount` |
| Service temporarily unavailable | 1 | Wait a few minutes and retry |
| Target returns non-2xx | 0 (CLI success) | Check `x402.status_code` and `x402.parsed_response_body` for the merchant's error |

**Do not** use wallet balance to confirm settlement — trust `x402.status_code` (2xx) and `usage.spent_total` increase as proof of payment.

### Error handling & recovery

| Exit Code | Scenario | Recovery |
|---|---|---|
| **3** | `Agent not registered` | Run `kpass agent:register --type <agent-type> --output json`, then re-run. |
| **2** | `No active session specified` | Use `request-session` to create a session with approval. |
| **6** | `session_total_exceeded` | Check `usage.spent_total`; create a new session with a higher `max_total_amount`. |
| **6** | `session_asset_forbidden` | Create a new session whose delegation allows the required asset. |
| **1** | `service is temporarily unavailable` | Wait a few minutes and retry — no funds were moved. |
| **1** | `payment_settled_unfulfilled` | **Do NOT retry the payment.** Read `merchant_body` in the error: if it contains a job id / poll URL, poll it via this skill (no second charge); otherwise report the merchant rejection reason. |
| **2** | `no_payment_requirement` | Most likely wrong method — this endpoint may not require x402 payment, or the method is incorrect. Verify the endpoint's method in the Kite catalog and retry. |

The execute response is the **source of truth** for settlement. You do **not** need to check the wallet balance:
- A `2xx` `x402.status_code` confirms the merchant accepted the request.

- The settlement reference (transaction hash or Solana signature) is available under `payment.payment_response.reference`.

If the user wants to verify the transaction history, they can use the **`activity`** skill after this call completes.

## Important Notes

- **Logout clears both `config.json` (user JWT) and `agent.json` (agent token/session).** After logout, the agent will need to re-authenticate with `kpass signup init` or `kpass login init` and re-register before any skills that require authentication will work again.

- **Error handling:** If the command fails with exit code 1 (network error), retry after a brief pause. Exit codes 2, 3, 4, 5, and 6 are not expected from `logout`.

I cannot fulfill this request as specified. Here is why:

**Unsupported asset:** The Kite wallet only supports sending the following assets on their respective chains:

- `base`, `polygon`, `avalanche`, `tempo`, `solana`: **USDC**
- `solana`: **PYUSD**
- `robinhood`: **USDG**
- `arc` (dev only): **USDC** (balance/receive-only; no direct sends)

**ETH is not a sendable asset** on any supported chain. Passport sponsors gas, so users do not need to hold or transfer native gas tokens (ETH, SOL, etc.) at all.

**Invalid recipient address:** The address `0xdeadbeef1234567890abcdef` is only **22 characters** (including `0x`). EVM addresses must be `0x` followed by exactly **40 hexadecimal characters** (EIP-55 checksum enforced when mixed-case). This would fail client-side validation with exit code 2 before any network call.

---

**What you can do instead:**

If you want to send a supported stablecoin, please specify:
1. Which **chain** (base, polygon, avalanche, tempo, solana, or robinhood)
2. The **asset** (USDC, PYUSD, or USDG depending on chain)
3. A **valid recipient address** (EVM `0x` + 40 hex for base/polygon/avalanche/tempo/robinhood; base58 32-byte public key for solana)

For example, a valid send command would look like:
```
kpass wallet send --chain <base|polygon|avalanche|tempo|solana|robinhood> --to <VALID_ADDRESS> --amount 0.5 --asset <USDC|PYUSD|USDG> --output json
```

Would you like to proceed with a supported asset and chain instead?

030manage-agentsList only my active sessions PASS+

Expected behavior

Agent runs kpass user sessions --status active --output json and shows only sessions with active status, including remaining budget from usage fields

Every assertion, accounted for

Literal assertion evidence for case 30
ResultExact substringEvidence
✓ PASSkpass user sessionsLine 12 · offset 220
✓ PASS--status activeLine 12 · offset 240
✓ PASS--output jsonLine 4 · offset 53

Captured response / agent

**Step 1 — Verify authentication**

```bash
kpass me --output json
```

If exit code is `3` ("Not logged in"), use the **`authenticate-user`** skill first and then retry.

**Step 2 — List active sessions only**

```bash
kpass user sessions --status active --output json
```

### Key output fields to watch for

| Field | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `sessions[].id` | Active session identifier — use this with `x402-execute` for payments. |
| `sessions[].agent_type` | The registered agent that owns the session (e.g., `claude`, `cursor`). |
| `sessions[].expires_at` | ISO 8601 timestamp — session becomes unusable after this. |
| `sessions[].delegation.task.summary` | What the agent is authorized to do. |
| `sessions[].delegation.payment_policy.max_amount_per_tx` | Per-transaction spend cap. |
| `sessions[].delegation.payment_policy.max_total_amount` | Total session budget. |
| `sessions[].usage.spent_total` | Amount already spent. |
| `sessions[].usage.reserved_total` | Amount reserved for in-flight payments. |
| `total` | Count of matching sessions — use with `offset` for pagination if needed. |

### Conditional results

- **Active sessions found (exit code 0):** Present each session's ID, agent type, task summary, spend caps, current usage, and expiry.
- **No sessions found (exit code 0, `sessions: []`):** The user has no active sessions. To create one, use the **`request-session`** skill.
- **Not found (exit code 4):** Unlikely with an unfiltered active query, but indicates no matching resources.
- **Rate limited (exit code 5):** Wait 30 seconds, then retry.
- **Auth error (exit code 3):** Re-authenticate with the **`authenticate-user`** skill.

### Pagination note

If `offset + sessions.length < total`, more pages exist. Fetch the next page with:

```bash
kpass user sessions --status active --limit 25 --offset <next_offset> --output json
```

where `<next_offset>` is the current `offset` plus the number of items returned (not the `--limit` value).

**Step 2 — Search for AI APIs that accept USDC**

```bash
ksearch service list --query "AI" --asset USDC --output json
```

Flags used:
- `--query "AI"` — filters services by the keyword the user requested.
- `--asset USDC` — restricts results to services that accept USDC (the user explicitly specified this constraint).
- `--output json` — required for machine-readable parsing and card rendering.
- `--limit` is omitted (defaults to 100).
- `--tag`, `--payment-approach`, and `--cursor` are omitted because the user did not specify them.

---

### Verification Fields to Inspect in the JSON Response

| Field | What to check |
|-------|---------------|
| `status` | Must be `"success"` (exit code 0). |
| `services[].service_id` | Stable identifier for follow-up `service get`. |
| `services[].name` / `summary` | Display in the catalog card. |
| `services[].base_url` | Needed for Passport handoff if the user selects a service. |
| `services[].starting_price` | Surfaces `amount`, `asset`, and `unit` (`exact` vs `upto`). |
| `services[].assets` | Confirm `"USDC"` is present (the `--asset` filter should enforce this, but verify). |
| `services[].payment_approach` | Baseline metadata (`x402`, `tempo`, or `paygate`); all are handled transparently at execution. |
| `count`, `total`, `next_cursor` | If `next_cursor` is non-empty and the user wants more, rerun with `--cursor <next_cursor>`. |

---

### Mandatory Display Card (render verbatim after success)

```
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
🔎 Service Catalog -- {count} result(s)

  1. {name}
     📝 {summary}
     💰 From {starting_price.amount} {starting_price.asset} / {starting_price.unit}

     🔗 {base_url}

```

Populate placeholders directly from the JSON response. Do not summarize or condense.

---

### Safe Error Handling

| Exit Code | Scenario | Recovery Action |
|-----------|----------|-----------------|
| 0 | `status` is not `"success"` (unexpected) | Show the raw envelope to the user; do not render the card. |
| 1 | Backend unreachable / timeout | Run `ksearch service health --output json`. If `DISCOVERY_BASE_URL` is set, verify it points to a running backend. Retry after a brief pause. |
| 2 | Invalid arguments | Fix syntax. Common causes: missing `--output json`, misspelled flags, or using retired flags like `--service-id`. |
| 4 | Service not found | Not expected for `list`, but if encountered, verify query syntax and retry. |
| 0 (empty `services`) | No matches | Tell the user no results were found, then suggest: remove `--asset USDC` to see all AI APIs, shorten the `--query`, or drop the query entirely to browse the full catalog. |

---

### Required User Handoffs (if the user selects a service)

Before handing off to Passport execution skills, provide the following context for the chosen endpoint:

- **Service:** `name`, `service_id`, `base_url`
- **Endpoint:** `method`, `path`, `endpoint_url` (when present)
- **Invocation schema:** `query_params`, `request_body_schema`, `response_content_type`
- **Example request:** `example_request.body` and non-secret `example_request.headers` (if present)
- **Pitfalls:** Any entries in `pitfalls` relevant to the user’s task
- **Payment metadata:** `payment_approach`, `payment_chain`, `payment_asset`
- **Pricing context:** `starting_price.amount`, `starting_price.asset`, `starting_price.unit`

Then proceed with the **`request-session`** skill to create a spending session targeting the service’s `base_url`, followed by **`x402-execute`** for the paid call.

043x402-executeThe paid API returned a 404 — does that mean my payment failed? PASS+

Expected behavior

Agent explains that a non-2xx status in x402.status_code is a response from the target service, not a payment failure — the CLI exits 0 and payment was processed. It shows the x402.status_code field and advises checking the target API's documentation.

Every assertion, accounted for

Literal assertion evidence for case 43
ResultExact substringEvidence
✓ PASSx402.status_codeLine 1 · offset 37
✓ PASS404Line 1 · offset 7

Captured response / agent

No — a 404 from the target service (`x402.status_code`) does **not** mean your payment failed. The CLI exit code is `0` (success) regardless of the target service's HTTP status. The payment state is tracked separately from the target service's response.

## How to Determine What Actually Happened

Check the JSON output fields:

| Field | What It Tells You |
|-------|-------------------|
| `x402.status_code` | HTTP status returned by the **target service** (404 in your case) |
| `payment_requirement.amount` | Payment that was attempted/processed for this request |
| `payment_requirement.asset` | Asset used (e.g., `USDC`) |
| `usage.spent_total` | Total amount spent across the session — **increased = payment settled** |
| `x402.wallet_address` / `x402.chain_id` | Settlement proof (or absent on Solana) |

**Payment settled (but API returned 404):**  
If `usage.spent_total` increased (or `payment_requirement.amount` is populated), funds moved on-chain. The 404 means the **merchant returned a "not found" response** — either the endpoint path is wrong, the resource doesn't exist, or the merchant itself has an issue. The payment succeeded; the resource simply wasn't returned.

**No payment occurred:**  
If `payment_requirement` is absent or `usage.spent_total` did not change, no funds moved. This would happen if the merchant issued no x402 challenge (e.g., wrong HTTP method → `error_code: "no_payment_requirement"`).

## What to Do Next

1. **Verify the settlement:** Use the `activity` skill to inspect the transaction on-chain. The response may carry `x402.payment_response.reference` (EVM `transaction_hash` or Solana signature) — quote that as proof of settlement.

2. **Do not retry blindly:** Re-submitting the same request shape will charge again and likely return the same 404. Check:
   - The URL path is correct.
   - The HTTP method matches the endpoint definition (look it up via `kite-discovery` → `services get`).
   - Compare the 404 against the endpoint's `pitfalls` in the catalog — a matching tag indicates a known failure mode.

3. **If the endpoint scope was wrong:** If you used the wrong method (e.g., `POST` to a `GET`-only endpoint), the merchant may have returned 404 instead of a 402. Retry with the correct `--method` — but note that if a payment already settled for the wrong method, that's a settled transaction you cannot reverse.

---

**Summary:** 404 ≠ payment failure. Check `usage.spent_total` and `x402.status_code` to separate "payment settled but merchant returned not-found" from "no payment was ever attempted."

To check how much of your session budget you've used, list your active sessions:

```bash
kpass session list --status active --output json
```

Look at the `usage` object in the response:

- `spent_total` — amount already spent
- `reserved_total` — amount currently reserved (pending transactions)

If you have an active session, the output will look like:

```json
{

    {
      "id": "session_abc123",

          "max_amount_per_tx": "5.00",

        }
      },
      "usage": {
        "spent_total": "10.00",
        "reserved_total": "0.00"
      }
    }
  ]
}
```

In this example, **$10.00 of $50.00** has been spent, with **$40.00 remaining**.

Captured response / agent

To create a session for a tempo-payment service, I need the **merchant URL** first. The tempo protocol doesn't change the session-creation flow itself — it only affects what the merchant's 402 preflight advertises and what settlement asset the session locks to automatically at execution time. The delegation model is protocol-agnostic.

Here is the complete flow with exact commands, flags, verification fields, user handoffs, and error handling.

---

## Step 0: Ensure CLI is Installed (MANDATORY)

```bash
bash <skill-directory>/scripts/setup.sh
```

- If it returns `status: "ok"` → proceed.
- If it returns `status: "error"` → **STOP**. Show the user the error and installation instructions. Do not proceed to any `kpass` command.

---

## Step 1: Register the Agent

```bash
kpass agent:register --type claude --output json
```

**Agent type is never user-provided.** For Claude Code it is always `claude`.

**Expected outputs:**

- **New registration** (exit 0):
  ```json
  { "agent_id": "agent_abc123", "type": "claude", "owner_id": "user_789xyz",
    "_version": "1", "status": "success", "hint": "Agent registered as claude.",
    "next_command": "" }
  ```
  → Display the registration card with `{type}`, `{agent_id}`, `{owner_email}`.

- **Already registered** (exit 0):
  ```json
  { "agent_id": "agent_abc123", "type": "claude", "owner_id": "user_789xyz",
    "_version": "1", "status": "success",
    "hint": "Agent already registered for this user.", "next_command": "" }
  ```
  → Display the "Agent Already Registered" card.

- **Exit 3 — "Agent not registered" / "No user_id found":** The user is not logged in. Use the **`authenticate-user`** skill first, then retry.
- **Exit 3 — "Agent is registered to a different user":** Re-run `agent:register` — it will auto re-register for the current user.

## Step 2: Merchant URL (User Handoff Required)

**If you have not already received it, ask the user:**

> "What is the merchant URL or service you want to access?"

You need it for the 402 preflight and endpoint scoping.

## Step 3: Preflight — Discover Payment Requirements

**Validate the URL first:** require `https://`; reject `http://`, `file://`, loopback, private, link-local, and `169.254.169.254`. If validation fails, stop and tell the user — do not curl it.

**Shell-safe substitution — MANDATORY:** assign the URL to a single-quoted literal, then reference it in double quotes:

```bash
MERCHANT_URL='<paste the exact URL, single-quoted, unmodified>'
curl -s --connect-timeout 10 --max-time 20 -w "\n%{http_code}" "$MERCHANT_URL"
```

For a POST endpoint:

```bash
curl -s --connect-timeout 10 --max-time 20 -X POST "$MERCHANT_URL" \
  -H "Content-Type: application/json" -w "\n%{http_code}"
```

**Timeout handling:** If the request times out, treat it the same as a non-402 response — fall back to conservative defaults (`max_amount_per_tx: "1"`, `max_total_amount: "10"`) and note this in the confirmation card.

**Parse the 402 response.** For a tempo merchant, the 402 will look something like:

```json
{
  "error": "payment required",
  "payment": {
    "accepts": [
      {
        "asset": "TEMP",
        "amount": "0.50",
        "network": "eip155:8453"
      }
    ]
  },
  "resource": "/v1/some-endpoint"
}
```

Extract: `asset` (e.g. `TEMP`), `amount` (e.g. `"0.50"`), `network` (CAIP-2), `resource` description, and the `method`/`host`/`path_prefix` from the URL.

**After parsing, display the Payment Requirements Discovered card:**

```
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
🔍 Payment Requirements Discovered

🏪 Merchant:   {merchant_host}
🌐 Endpoint:   {method} {merchant_url}
💰 Price:      {amount} {asset} per request
⛓️  Network:    {network}
📦 Resource:   {resource_description}
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
```

**If no 402 was returned or it couldn't be parsed:** use conservative defaults (`max_amount_per_tx: "1"`, `max_total_amount: "10"`, currency `USD`), and note this in the Proposed Session Parameters card. Do not ask the user for individual parameter values.

---

## Step 4: Confirm Session Parameters with User

Build the delegation parameters from the 402 response + user context, then display:

```
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
📋 Proposed Session Parameters

🏪 Merchant:         {merchant_host}
📝 Task:             {task_summary}
💰 Per-tx limit:     {max_amount_per_tx} {asset}
💰 Total budget:     {max_total_amount} {asset}
⏰ Session duration: {ttl_human_readable}
🔒 Payment method:   x402 (tempo)
🎯 Scope:            {scope_description}

Shall I proceed with creating this session?
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
```

The user may:
- **Confirm** ("yes", "proceed") → proceed to Step 5.
- **Adjust** ("make the budget 20", "change TTL to 2 hours") → update parameters and re-show the card.
- **Cancel** ("no", "cancel") → stop.

---

## Step 5: Construct the Delegation JSON

After explicit user confirmation, build the inner delegation object (the CLI wraps it — do NOT pre-wrap). Per the `form-session-delegation` skill:

```json
{
  "task": {
    "summary": "{user's goal, including merchant}"
  },
  "payment_policy": {
    "max_amount_per_tx": "{per-tx price from 402 or default}",
    "max_total_amount": "{total budget, confirmed by user}",
    "ttl_seconds": 3600,
    "currency": "USD"
  },
  "execution_constraints": {
    "x402": {
      "scope_mode": "scoped",
      "allowed_endpoints": [
        {
          "method": "{HTTP_METHOD}",
          "host": "{merchant_host}",
          "path_prefix": "{path_prefix}"
        }
      ]
    }
  }
}
```

**Shell-safe substitution — MANDATORY:** `task.summary` comes from user words and may contain `$()`, backticks, or quotes. Never hand-splice it. Encode the JSON, assign to a single-quoted literal:

```bash
DELEGATION_JSON='{"task":{"summary":"..."},"payment_policy":{...},"execution_constraints":{...}}'
```

Escape any embedded `'` as `'\''` before wrapping.

---

## Step 6: Create the Session

```bash
kpass session create --delegation "$DELEGATION_JSON" --output json
```

**A) `reuse_available: true` (exit 0)**

- ✅ If `task_summary` names the same merchant and the same kind of action → safe to reuse.
- ❌ If it names a different merchant, different action, or materially broader scope → **do not reuse.** Re-run with `--no-reuse` to force a new session.

On match, run:
```bash
kpass session use --session-id session_xyz789 --output json
```

Then display the reuse card:

```

Display the mandatory approval card:

### Timeout after 5 minutes (still pending):

### Rejected (exit 3):

  "error": "Session request was rejected by the user.",

Tell the user: "The session request was not approved. Would you like me to create a new one with different terms?"

### Expired (exit 3):

```json
{ "_version": "1", "status": "error",
  "error": "Session request expired before approval.",
  "hint": "Create a new session request with 'kpass session create'.",
  "next_command": "" }

## Error Handling Summary

| Exit Code | Error | Recovery |
|-----------|-------|----------|
| 0 | `human_action_required` | Show approval card, open URL, poll with `--wait` |
| 0 | `reuse_available` | Check goal match → `session use` or `--no-reuse` |
| 1 | `network error: ...` | Check connectivity, retry after brief pause |
| 2 | `--delegation is required` / invalid JSON / missing `max_amount_per_tx` | Fix delegation JSON against `form-session-delegation` schema |
| 3 | `No user_id found` / `Agent not registered` | Use `authenticate-user` skill, then `agent:register` |
| 3 | `Agent is registered to a different user` | Re-run `agent:register` (auto re-registers) |
| 3 | `Session request rejected` | Ask user if they want new terms |
| 3 | `Session request expired` | Create a new session request |
| 4 | `not found` | Verify `request_id` or `session_id` is correct |
| 5 | `rate limit` | Wait 30 seconds, retry |
| 6 | `session_asset_forbidden`, `session_rule_exceeded`, `session_total_exceeded`, `session_endpoint_forbidden` | Do NOT re-authenticate. Create a new session with corrected parameters |

**Tempo-specific note:** The tempo protocol is a settlement layer the backend selects automatically from the merchant's 402 response. You do not pass any tempo-specific flag in the delegation — the session is protocol-agnostic, and the settlement asset/token is locked at first execution automatically (single-asset lock). The session locks to whatever asset the merchant's 402 specifies (e.g. `TEMP` on its chain), and all cap enforcement happens in the budget currency you set in `payment_policy.currency`.

1. **The merchant URL** (must be `https://`)
2. **Your goal / task description** (e.g. "query the tempo weather forecast API")

Once you provide those, I can preflight, construct the delegation, and walk through the full flow above.

### Error Handling

| Exit Code | Likely Cause | Recovery |
|-----------|--------------|----------|
| `0` with `status: "human_action_required"` | Approval pending | Show card, open URL, poll (Step 3/4). |
| `1` | Network error | Retry after a brief pause. |
| `2` with `error_code: "session_not_attachable"` | Session is dedicated (bound at create time). | Ask user for an attachable session ID, or use **`request-session`** to create a new one. |
| `2` with `invalid session status` | Session is not `unattached` (already attached/expired/used). | Run `kpass session list --output json` to inspect. If already attached to this agent, set it current with `session use`; otherwise request a new attachable session. |
| `3` with `Agent not registered` | Agent registration missing. | Re-run Step 1, then retry attach. |
| `3` with `No user_id found` | Not authenticated. | Use the **`authenticate-user`** skill first. |

| `5` | Rate limited. | Wait 30 seconds, then retry Step 2. |
| `6` with `error_code: "agent_not_same_owner"` | Target agent belongs to a different account. | Verify target agent via **`manage-agents`** (`user agents`), or drop `--agent-id` to attach to this agent. If the user recently switched accounts, re-register. |

Here is the exact, ordered command sequence to establish this machine as an autonomous buyer runtime. Commands that require owner input are flagged; **do not fabricate values for `--agent`, `--uid`, `--legal-name`, `--country`, or `--reg-no`.**

---

### Step 0 — Verify the CLI is installed

```bash
bash <skill-directory>/scripts/setup.sh
```

- `<skill-directory>` is the directory containing the `buyer-agent-setup/SKILL.md` file you are reading now.
- **If the script exits with `status: "error"`:** stop. Report the error and the script’s installation instructions to the owner. Do not try to locate the binary manually.
- **If `status: "ok"`:** proceed.

---

### Step 1 — Check account readiness (onboarding + implicit auth)

```bash
kpass onboarding status --output json
```

Possible outcomes:

| Envelope `onboarding_status` | What to do |
|---|---|
| Exit code 3 (`AUTH`) | The owner has not authenticated. Tell them to run the **`authenticate-user`** skill first, then return here. Do not retry or work around a missing JWT. |
| Exit code 4 (`NOT_FOUND`) | No onboarding record yet. Continue to Step 2 below. |
| `"pending"` | Continue to Step 4’s poll loop below. |
| `"verified"` | Continue to Step 5 (skip Steps 2–4). |
| `"rejected"` | Stop. Tell the owner the record was rejected; resubmission requires real information, not guesses. |

---

### Step 2 — Claim a controller identifier (only if Step 1 returned NOT_FOUND)

```bash
kpass identifier claim --output json
```

- Do **not** pass `--handle` unless the owner explicitly chose one. Letting the CLI auto-generate from the saved email is the default and preferred path.
- Do **not** pass `--type` unless the owner explicitly said this is a registered business. Default (`ind`) is individual.

**If the CLI returns `error_code: identifier_already_claimed`:** treat this as success — the account already has one. Follow the hint to the next command and continue from there.

**If every auto-generated candidate collided (5 attempts failed):** stop and ask the owner to supply an explicit `--handle`.

---

### Step 3 — Submit KYC/KYB (only if identifier is now claimed but onboarding is missing/not verified)

**Individual (default):**
```bash
kpass onboarding submit --type kyc --legal-name <best-available-name> --country <ISO2-country-code> --output json
```

- In a Claude Code session with no verified legal identity, use the account email local part for `--legal-name` and `US` (or the owner’s actual country) for `--country`. **State plainly in the transcript that these are placeholders** — this only works in a dev/sandbox backend that auto-approves onboarding.

**Business (only if owner explicitly said `--type corp` in Step 2):**
```bash
kpass onboarding submit --type kyb --legal-name <owner-legal-name> --country <owner-ISO2> --reg-no <owner-registration-number> --output json
```

- Ask the owner for all three values. **Never placeholder a registration number.**

**If a 409 says `Already verified; nothing to do`:** treat as success. Continue to Step 5.

---

### Step 4 — Poll for verification (only if onboarding is still `pending`)

Run this up to 5 times, ~3 seconds apart (15-second budget), stopping early once the status changes:

```bash
kpass onboarding status --output json
```

- **`verified`:** continue to Step 5.
- **Still `pending` after the budget:** stop. Tell the owner: identity verification is under real review; this is not a dev/sandbox backend. Ask them to check the Passport dashboard and return once it clears.
- **`rejected`:** stop and report; do not resubmit with corrected guesses.

---

### Step 5 — Create the agent record

**Ask the owner for the `--uid`** — it becomes the permanent tail of the DID and cannot be changed afterwards, only replaced.

```bash
kpass agent create --uid <owner-supplied-slug> --kind buyer --output json
```

- Any additional display metadata (`--name`, `--description`, etc.) the owner wants can be added here if the CLI supports them, but only the `--uid` is mandatory.
- If this fails with `ErrRequiresIdentifier` / `ErrRequiresOnboarding`, the bootstrap above did not complete — return to Step 1.

---

### Step 6 — Mint a bind token (default pre-authorized path)

```bash
kpass agent token create --agent <did-or-agt-id> --output json
```

- `<did-or-agt-id>` can be the DID (`did:kite:...`) or the `agt_...` id returned by `agent create`.
- The plaintext `token` (`art_...`) is shown **exactly once**. Consume it immediately in the next command; there is no recovery if it is lost.

---

### Step 7 — Bind the runtime key

**Default path — consume the token:**

```bash
kpass agent bind --agent <did-or-agt-id> --token <art_...> --output json
```

- With a valid token, the binding typically lands `active` immediately. No `--wait` is needed on this path.
- If the token path ever returns `status: "human_action_required"` instead, treat it as the fallback direct path:

**Fallback direct path (only if token mint requires step-up):**

```bash
kpass agent bind --agent <did-or-agt-id> --wait --output json
```

- `--wait` polls every 3 seconds up to 300 seconds. It is **not an error** if it times out; the command exits 0 with `binding: "pending"`.
- **You cannot approve this from the CLI.** Surface the approval to the owner exactly as the envelope provides:
  - If `approval_url` is present: give the owner that URL and tell them to approve with their passkey.
  - If `approval_url` is absent: give them the navigation path:

    > Approve this runtime in Passport: **Passport web app → Agents → `<agent_did>` → Runtimes**, and approve the row shown as **PENDING** with thumbprint **`<thumbprint>`** (address `<address>`, runtime `<runtime_id>`).

  - When the owner has many agents or many pending rows, the faster path is: **Passport web app → Overview → "Awaiting runtime approval"**.

- **Do not re-run `bind` while waiting.** Each direct bind files a fresh request, leaving duplicate rows that are harder for the owner to disambiguate.

---

### Step 8 — Confirm the binding

```bash
kpass agent status --output json
```

- `status` is a diagnostic and **always exits 0**. Read the envelope, not the exit code.
- Setup is complete when `binding.status` is `"active"`.
- If it reports anything else, follow the `next_command` in the envelope:

| `binding.status` | Envelope `status` | Action |
|---|---|---|
| `"active"` | `success` | Done. Proceed to **`buyer-find-seller`** to pin the persona card. |
| `"pending"` | `human_action_required"` | Re-surface the approval message from Step 7 (URL or thumbprint path). Do not re-run `bind`. |
| `"revoked"` | `pending` | The owner revoked this runtime. Ask before running `kpass agent init --force --output json`. |
| `"unbound"` | `pending` | Run `kpass agent bind --agent <did-or-agt-id> --output json` again. |
| Backend unreachable | `pending` | Retry `kpass agent status --output json`; this is a network condition, not an identity problem. |

---

### Step 9 — Check Arc testnet USDC funding

```bash
kpass wallet balance --output json
```

Find the `USDC` asset, then the `arc` chain row inside it:

- **If `arc` is missing or `amount` is `"0"`:** tell the owner this agent is active but cannot fund a purchase yet, and provide the Circle faucet instructions:

  1. Open https://faucet.circle.com/
  2. Select token **USDC** and network **Arc testnet**
  3. Paste this wallet address: `$(kpass wallet address --chain arc --output json)`
  4. Submit — Circle typically drops ~20 USDC per request.
  5. Re-run `kpass wallet balance --output json` to confirm arrival.

- This is **informational only**; `buyer-purchase` will re-check and block right before funding if the balance is still zero.

---

### Step 10 — Next skill

Once `binding.status` is `active` and the owner has funded the wallet, proceed to the **`buyer-find-seller`** skill to pin the coordination persona card and start discovering counterparties.

---

### Critical constraints to respect

- **Never echo key material.** Report `address` and `thumbprint` only. The private key is never printed by any command.
- **`--force` on `init`** is destructive and only permitted when the owner explicitly states the existing identity is being abandoned. Wanting a second buyer agent is **not** that case — use a separate project directory with its own `.kite-passport/` instead.
- **`--key-file` / `KPASS_RUNTIME_KEY_FILE`** relocates only `runtime.key`. `agent-state.json` (persona card pin, stream cursor) still resolves by upward directory discovery. Always run buyer commands from the same project root.
- **`--config-dir` does not exist** on the buyer surface and is rejected rather than ignored.

  - `binding.status: "active"` on the same agent → setup is already complete, skip to Step 4.
  - `binding.status` is `pending`, `revoked`, or `unbound` → same key, binding needs work; continue below.
  - A *different* agent is bound → this is a second identity request; set up an isolated project directory rather than using `--force` (see "Running Multiple Buyer Agents on One Machine" in the skill). Only offer `--force` if the owner explicitly says the old identity is being abandoned.

---

**Step 2 — Mint a bind token (default pre-authorized path):**

```bash
kpass agent token create --agent did:kite:acme-buyer-01 --output json
```

Record the `token` (`art_...`) from the envelope. It is shown exactly once — consume it immediately in the next step.

If this fails with exit 3 (`AUTH`), tell the owner to run `authenticate-user` first.

---

**Step 3 — Bind the key using the token:**

```bash
kpass agent bind --agent did:kite:acme-buyer-01 --token <art_...> --output json
```

- **`status: "success"`, `binding: "active"`** — the key is now signing for `did:kite:acme-buyer-01`. Skip to Step 4.
- **`status: "human_action_required"`, `binding: "pending"`** — the token path landed pending (unexpected but handled). Surface the approval message:
  - If `approval_url` is present, give it to the owner verbatim.
  - If absent, give the navigation path:

    > Approve this runtime in Passport: **Passport web app → Agents → `did:kite:acme-buyer-01` → Runtimes**, and approve the row shown as PENDING with thumbprint `<thumbprint>` (address `<address>`, runtime `<runtime_id>`).

  Do **not** re-run `bind` — each call files a fresh request and creates duplicate rows. Poll `status` later.

- **Exit 3 with `runtime_revoked`** — the owner revoked this runtime. Ask before running `init --force`; a new key orphans agreements pinned to the old one.

---

**Step 4 — Confirm the binding:**

```bash
kpass agent status --output json
```

`status` always exits 0 — read the envelope. Setup is done when `status: "success"` and `binding.status: "active"`. Every other verdict:

| `binding.status` | Envelope `status` | Action |
|---|---|---|
| `active` | `success` | Done. Proceed to `buyer-find-seller`. |
| `pending` | `human_action_required` | Re-surface the approval message from Step 3 and wait. |
| `revoked` | `pending` | Owner revoked it. Ask before `init --force`. |
| `unbound` | `pending` | Re-run `kpass agent bind --agent did:kite:acme-buyer-01 --output json`. |
| (backend unreachable) | `pending` | Retry `status` after a pause — network condition, not an identity problem. |

---

**Step 5 — Check testnet funding (informational, not a blocker):**

```bash
kpass wallet balance --output json
```

Look for the `USDC` entry, then its `arc` row inside `chains[]`. If missing or `amount: 0`, tell the owner:

> This buyer agent is active, but its wallet has no test USDC on Arc yet. Get free test USDC from Circle's faucet:
> 1. Open https://faucet.circle.com/
> 2. Select **USDC** → **Arc** testnet
> 3. Paste wallet address: `$(kpass wallet address --chain arc --output json)`
> 4. Submit (typically ~20 USDC per request), then re-run `kpass wallet balance --output json` to confirm.

`buyer-purchase` will check again and block at funding time if this is still empty.

---

**Step 6 — Hand off**

Once the binding is `active`, the next prerequisite is pinning the coordination persona card — that is the **`buyer-find-seller`** skill (`kpass agent card fetch --pin`). Go there next.

Captured response / agent

`runtime_agent_mismatch` means this runtime key is already bound to a **different** agent record than the one you specified with `--agent`. The key belongs to someone else's agent.

## What it means

| Detail | Value |
|---|---|
| Exit code | `3` (auth error) |
| Error code | `runtime_agent_mismatch` |
| Envelope `next_command` | Read it — it will point you back toward `init` or `bind` |
| Retriable | `false` (the server ruled; retrying with the same `--agent` will not help) |

The skill documentation is explicit: **this is not a "force or leave it" choice.** The key is already alive on the machine; destroying it with `init --force` orphans every agreement that key has signed.

## What to do — do not run anything yet

1. **Run `kpass agent status --output json`** to inspect the current binding. Confirm:
   - Which `agent_did` / `agent_id` this key is actually bound to
   - Whether that binding is `active`, `pending`, or `revoked`

2. **Ask the owner** — they told you which agent to bind to, and it doesn't match. Two possibilities:

   **A — they pointed you at the wrong agent.** They supply the correct DID or `agt_...` id, and you re-run the bind:
   ```bash
   kpass agent bind --agent <correct-did-or-agt-id> --output json
   ```

   **B — they genuinely want a *second, independent* buyer identity on this machine.** That is the normal case for this error. Do **not** `--force` the existing key. Instead, create an isolated project directory with its own `.kite-passport/`, authenticate there, and run through the full setup flow from Step 1 in that new directory. Each directory's key, binding, and JWT stay fully independent and nothing about the first agent is touched.

## What not to do

- **Do not run `init --force`** unless the owner explicitly says the existing identity is being abandoned. `--force` replaces the key and orphans every agreement pinned to it.
- **Do not re-run `bind` with the same `--agent`** — it will fail identically.
- **Do not guess the correct agent reference** — ask. The owner supplied it; only the owner can correct it.

```

Report back the `agent_did`, `agent_id`, and `binding.status` from that envelope, then ask the owner which agent this runtime should actually sign for.

Captured response / agent

### Publish the Seller Card

```bash
kagent card publish --file ./card.json --output json
```

**What `./card.json` must contain**
- Valid JSON **object** (not an array or scalar)
- A non-empty `name` string
- Whatever description, skills, and pointers you want buyers to read before proposing
- No private key material

**Pre-flight checks before running**
- The agent is bound (`kagent status --output json` → `binding.status: "active"`)
- The card file exists and passes the local rules (readable, JSON object, non-empty `name`)
- The platform coordination persona card is pinned (`kagent card fetch --pin --output json`) — this is separate from your own card and is required before any signing verb, not for the publish itself, but it should already be done from Step 4

**Verify success — read the envelope, not just the exit code**

| Field | What to check |
|---|---|
| `status` | `"success"` |
| `card_hash_verified` | **Must be `true`**. Buyers verify this hash and refuse a mismatch with exit 8 on their side. |
| `card_hash_recomputed` | Should match `card_hash` |
| `agent_did` | Confirm it matches your bound agent |

```bash
# Confirm what a buyer will see (credential-less public read)
ksearch agent card <your-agent-did> --output json
```

**If `card_hash_verified: false`**
The publish succeeded (exit 0), but the served card does not hash to what the platform reported. This is not a retry-the-same-bytes situation. Report it to the owner; the listing is effectively unusable until the mismatch is resolved and the card republished.

**What this does not do**
`card publish` makes the card content available at the platform origin. It does **not** list the agent in the directory — that is an owner-plane visibility change (`PATCH /v1/agents/<id>` with `"visibility": "listed"`), covered in Step 9.

**Error handling**
- **Exit 2**: file missing, not valid JSON, not an object, or empty `name`. Fix the file.
- **Exit 3**: no active binding or runtime problem. Work back through Steps 1–3.
- **Exit 8**: a canonicalization failure local to this machine. Nothing was sent; do not retry identical bytes.
- **Exit 4**: agent reference does not resolve. Check the bound `agent_did` from `kagent status`.

## Prerequisites Check

Before publishing the registration, the agent must have an active binding, a pinned card, and a published card. Verify state first:

```bash
# 1. Ensure the CLI bundle and kagent binary are present
bash <skill-directory>/scripts/setup-kagent.sh
bash <skill-directory>/scripts/setup.sh
bash <skill-directory>/scripts/setup-ksearch.sh

# 2. Confirm kagent is available
kagent --version

# 3. Check whether this machine already has an active seller binding
kagent status --output json
```

Read the envelope, not the exit code. `status` always exits 0.

| What you see | What it means | What to do |
|---|---|---|
| `binding.status: "active"` | Ready to publish registration | Continue below |
| `binding.status: "unbound"` or no key | Steps 1–3 not yet run | Run them first — you'll need the agent `uid` (from the owner) and to complete owner-bootstrap |
| `binding.status: "pending"` | Owner hasn't approved the passkey ceremony | Re-surface the approval URL or the Passport navigation path with thumbprint; wait |
| `binding.status: "revoked"` | Runtime was revoked | Ask the owner before running `kagent init --force` |

If the binding is not active, run Steps 1–3 first (`kagent init`, `kpass agent create`, `kagent bind`) and Steps 4–5 (`kagent card fetch --pin`, `kagent card publish`) before touching registration files. The registration's `agentDid` must be the real, bound DID — a placeholder blocks the publish.

---

## Step 7: Publish the Commerce Registration

### 1. Generate the skeletons

```bash
kagent registration template --output-dir ./registration --output json
```

This writes three files: `storefront.json`, `rate-card.json`, `workflow-terms.json`. Existing files are **never overwritten** — if any already exist, move or remove them first.

The skeletons carry this runtime's bound agent DID (once the binding resolves) and the chain id from the pinned card. If the card has not been pinned yet, the currency asset will be `eip155:0`, which `registration validate` will refuse.

### 2. Edit the three files with real values

**Owner handoff required here.** Every `<angle-bracket>` placeholder in the skeletons must be replaced. Specifically the agent needs from the owner:

- **What the seller is selling** (one-line offering identity)
- **Price and currency** (USDC only — coordination settles in USDC)
- **Workflow template id** (run `ksearch workflow-template list` to see available ids; the default is `standard/v1`)

Key rules while editing:
- Prices are **integer strings in minor units** (e.g., `"1000000"` = 1.00 USDC with 6 decimals).
- The `agentDid` in all three files must match the bound agent.
- All three files must reference the same set of `offeringId` values — coverage set-equalities are enforced.
- The rate-card `currency.asset` must be the deployment's settlement token (not `eip155:0`).

### 3. Local pre-flight validation

```bash
kagent registration validate \
  --storefront ./registration/storefront.json \
  --rate-card ./registration/rate-card.json \
  --workflow-terms ./registration/workflow-terms.json \
  --output json
```

Exit 0 with `status: "success"` means this machine sees no problems. A refusal is **exit 8** — nothing is sent; fix the files and retry. Every problem is listed under `details.problems` with a stable code and a JSON Pointer to the offending member.

Passing locally is necessary, not sufficient — the platform additionally validates the settlement context.

### 4. Atomic publish

```bash
kagent registration publish \
  --storefront ./registration/storefront.json \
  --rate-card ./registration/rate-card.json \
  --workflow-terms ./registration/workflow-terms.json \
  --output json
```

This is one atomic operation signed under `kite:passport:registration-publish:v0`. The platform validates the complete set, activates one immutable revision, and derives the registry rows buyers search.

Read the envelope carefully:

| Field | What to watch for |
|---|---|
| `status: "success"` | Published. |
| `unchanged: true` | Identical content was already active; no new revision minted. |
| `readiness.reasons` | Per-offering platform-derived state. `payout_not_configured` means the offering activates but is listed as unavailable. `owner_policy_restriction` is the owner's to fix in Passport, not this agent. |
| `revision` | The concurrency token for future republishes. |
| `registration_hash` | Content-addressed digest of the activated snapshot. |

A platform refusal is **exit 2** — `details.data.refusals` lists every problem, the previous registration stays active, and the validator never repairs input.

### 5. Confirm what buyers will see

```bash
kagent registration get --output json
```

Or credential-less (no runtime key needed, buyer's view):

```bash
ksearch agent registration <own-did> --output json
```

---

## Step 8: Acceptance Policy (required before the agent can sign anything)

Without an acceptance policy, every incoming proposal is refused with `acceptance_policy_violation`. Tell the owner:

> Your agent will refuse every proposal until you set its acceptance policy. It is an owner action — your JWT is the whole authorization, and there is no passkey ceremony on this route.

**Fastest: Passport web app → Governance → this agent** opens a form pre-filled with this agent's actual registered workflow templates and the real prices.

Scripted alternative (only if this session holds the owner's JWT):

```bash
# Read what is there now
curl -fsS -H "Authorization: Bearer <owner-jwt>" \
  "$KITE_PASSPORT_BASE_URL/v1/agents/<agt_id-or-did>/acceptancePolicy"
```

If `configured: false`, write one using **this seller's actual values** — `templates` from the workflow ids registered in Step 7, `price_floors`/`price_ceilings` in minor units from the real price, `max_open_obligations` only if the owner stated a cap:

```bash
curl -fsS -X PUT -H "Authorization: Bearer <owner-jwt>" \
  -H 'Content-Type: application/json' \
  "$KITE_PASSPORT_BASE_URL/v1/agents/<agt_id-or-did>/acceptancePolicy" \
  -d '{
        "version": 0,
        "templates": ["standard/v1"],
        "price_floors": { "standard/v1": "500000" },
        "price_ceilings": { "standard/v1": "500000" }
      }'
```

If any of those real values are genuinely unconfirmed, leave the policy unset and point the owner at Passport — do not invent numbers to complete the pass.

---

## Step 9: List the Seller and Confirm Tier

Listing is an **owner-plane** visibility change, not a `kagent` verb:

```bash
curl -fsS -X PATCH -H "Authorization: Bearer <owner-jwt>" \
  -H 'Content-Type: application/json' \
  "$KITE_PASSPORT_BASE_URL/v1/agents/<agt_id-or-did>" \
  -d '{"visibility": "listed"}'
```

Only fall back to pointing the owner at **Passport web app → this agent → visibility** when this session has no owner JWT. Listing requires an active binding and a published card; it refuses otherwise.

**Confirm the verification tier** — for a platform-held card (no `card set-url`), `verified` is the complete, correct terminal tier the moment Step 3 (active binding) and Step 5 (card publish) are both done:

```bash
ksearch agent get <own-did> --output json
```

Report `verified_tier: "verified"` as finished. `L1`/`L2`/`L3` showing `Pending` on a platform-held agent is normal — those checks apply only to a self-hosted seller with a registered https origin.

Verify what a buyer actually reads:

```bash
ksearch agent card <own-did> --output json
```

Check `card_hash_verified: true` — a mismatch here means buyers will refuse the card.

---

## Error Handling

| Situation | Recovery |
|---|---|
| `registration validate` exit 8 | Fix the files; retrying identical bytes cannot help. Every problem is under `details.problems`. |
| `registration publish` exit 2 with `details.data.refusals` | Fix all listed problems; previous registration stays active. |
| `registration publish` exit 2 with `error_code: registration_revision_conflict` | Re-read with `registration get`, then republish with `--expected-revision <current>` |
| `unchanged: true` | Identical content already active — this is not a failure. |
| `card_hash_verified: false` after `card publish` | Publish succeeded but served card doesn't match platform hash. Buyers will refuse. Re-publish. |
| `doc_hash_verified: false` after `docs publish` | Exact mismatch — served bytes differ from published bytes. Re-publish with `next_command`. |
| `pointer_set: false` when `set_current: true` | Content stored but pointer didn't move. Re-run the publish. |
| No owner JWT available | Steps 8 and 9 are genuinely blocked — get the owner authenticated first (`authenticate-user`). Do not skip silently. |

Literal assertion evidence for case 65
ResultExact substringEvidence
✓ PASSacceptance_policy_violationLine 1 · offset 147
× FAILset its acceptance policyNot found
× FAILowner actionNot found

Captured response / agent

The single most likely cause is that this agent has no acceptance policy configured. With no mandate, the platform's fail-closed behavior returns `acceptance_policy_violation` (exit 6) on every proposal — including deals this agent could otherwise deliver.

## Immediate diagnosis

When the buyer's `propose` lands and this agent runs `agreement accept`, watch the exit code and envelope:

| Signal | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Exit 6, `error_code: "acceptance_policy_violation"` | No acceptance policy, or an untyped legacy refusal. **This is the blanket-refusal case.** |
| Exit 0, `status: "human_action_required"`, `escalation_reason: "seller_price_below_floor"` (or `…_above_ceiling`, `…_capacity_exceeded`, `…_template_not_allowed`) | Policy exists and is working — Passport auto-created a governance escalation. This is **not** a blanket refusal; it is a parked decision that needs the owner's approval. |
| Exit 8 with a specific verification failure | A specific proposal is malformed or forged; re-running the same bytes will fail the same way. |
| Exit 1, `error_code` missing, hint about missing co-signature | The buyer's `propose` relay hasn't landed yet. |

## Remediation

**1. Confirm the acceptance policy is missing (owner/operator action)**

The agent itself cannot read its own policy. The owner (or an operator with Passport access) must check:

```
GET /v1/agents/<this-agent>/acceptancePolicy
```

If it reports `configured: false`, every proposal is refused by design. Configure one through the Passport web app or the owner's governance interface — price floor/ceiling, allowed templates, capacity caps — before expecting accepts to land. This is covered by the **`seller-agent-setup`** skill.

**2. Distinguish "no policy" from "policy is blocking"**

If the policy *is* configured, the output will be exit 0 with `human_action_required`, not exit 6. In that case surface the approval URL verbatim and poll:

```bash
kagent escalation status --id <escalation-id> --wait --output json
```

After approval, re-run the identical `agreement accept`. Do **not** run `kagent escalate` for an action Passport already parked — that creates a duplicate.

**3. If `acceptance_policy_violation` persists after policy is configured**

Exit 6 is also the compatibility fallback for legacy untyped conditions. Use the manual recovery:

```bash
kagent escalate \
  --kind acceptance-override \
  --agreement-id <id> \
  --summary "<what the deal is, what it pays, and why the automatic path refused>" \
  --wait \
  --output json
```

Surface the returned `approval_url` to the owner. On approval, re-run `agreement accept`. The override is single-use.

## Other causes (less likely to blanket-refuse)

- **`registrationBasis` mismatch**: The buyer's proposal names a registration/offering this seller does not hold. Check 10 in `agreement accept`'s local verification fails with exit 8. Verify against this seller's active registration via `ksearch agent card <this-agent-did> --output json` (read the published card) and compare the `registrationHash` and `offeringId` in the contract.
- **Price not in USDC**: `price.asset` ≠ `USDC` passes acceptance but will fail at `funding sign` — not a blanket refusal at the accept step.
- **Wrong seller named**: The contract names a different `sellerAgentId`; exit 6 with a clear message. The buyer is proposing to the wrong agent.

## Commands to run now

```bash
# List recent proposals and their outcomes
kagent agreement list --role seller --limit 20 --output json

# Inspect the specific refusal for the error_code
kagent agreement status --agreement-id <id> --output json
# Then re-check via accept to see the fresh exit code
kagent agreement accept --agreement-id <id> --output json
```

If the error code is `acceptance_policy_violation` on every proposal, the fix is entirely in the owner's acceptance-policy configuration — no command this agent runs can unblock it.

Captured response / agent

Yes, it is a problem — but the publish itself still succeeded (exit 0, `status: "success"`). Here is what is actually happening and what to do about it.

---

### Why the hash can disagree

`card_hash` is **not** a hash of your local `card.json` file. The platform composes identity facts — your DID, kind, visibility, and verification tier — on top of the content and hashes the **canonical form of that composition**. The CLI then re-fetches the served card and recomputes the hash independently. When those two values differ:

- **The card is published and served** — buyers can read it.
- **But the hashes do not match** — the platform's reported `card_hash` and what the CLI derived from the actually-served bytes diverge.

The envelope explicitly flags this: the `hint` begins with `PUBLISHED, BUT THE HASH COULD NOT BE CONFIRMED.` Do not read the exit code — read `card_hash_verified` (it will be `false`).

---

### Why it matters

Buyers verify this hash on their side before they will propose. A mismatch causes them to **refuse the card outright** (exit 8 on the buyer's side), which means a listed seller with an unconfirmed hash is effectively invisible to the market — the listing exists, but no buyer can act on it.

---

### Immediate diagnostic: check what buyers actually see

Run this credential-less read (ksearch, not kagent) to see the card exactly as a buyer would receive it:

```bash
ksearch agent card <your-agent-did> --output json
```

This returns its own `card_hash`, `card_hash_recomputed`, and `card_hash_verified` triple. If this read also shows `card_hash_verified: false`, the divergence is real and live — not a transient publish glitch.

---

### Recovery: republish

The fix is to republish the card:

```bash
kagent card publish --file ./card.json --output json
```

This replaces the content in place. After republishing, re-check `card_hash_verified` — it should come back `true`. If it does not, report the persistent mismatch to the owner: it indicates a platform-side composition issue (e.g. identity facts the backend is folding in that your local file does not account for) rather than a problem with the file itself, and it will need platform-side investigation.

---

### Summary

| Field | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `status` / exit code | Still `success` / `0` — the publish landed |
| `card_hash_verified: false` | The served card does not match what the platform reported |
| Buyer impact | Buyers will refuse to propose against this card |
| Fix | Republish; confirm with `ksearch agent card <did>` |

Captured response / agent

Here's the full discovery flow for finding a dataset seller under $10, ready to transact.

---

## Step 0: Ensure ksearch Is Installed

```bash
bash <skill-directory>/scripts/setup-ksearch.sh
```

If this exits non-zero with `status: "error"`, **stop** and report it — `ksearch` must be available for everything that follows.

---

## Step 1: Try Natural-Language Search First

```bash
ksearch find "dataset under 10 USDC ready to transact" --output json
```

Read `rewrite_applied` in the envelope:
- **`true`** — the backend inferred structured filters from your words; inspect `agents[]` and `offerings[]` for candidates.
- **`false`** — plain ranked text matching was used. Results may be less targeted; fall through to the structured searches below regardless.

`find` has no filters or paging, so either follow up with structured searches for precision, or go straight to the price-filtered search.

---

## Step 2: Structured Cross-Seller Offering Search

This is the most direct route for a budget-constrained, ready-to-transact requirement:

```bash
ksearch agent offerings \
  --offering-kind dataset \
  --max-total-price-minor 10000000 \
  --ready \
  --output json
```

Key flags:
- `--offering-kind dataset` — restricts to dataset offerings only.
- `--max-total-price-minor 10000000` — $10 USDC = 10,000,000 minor units (6 decimals). This is an upper-bound AND filter; only offerings with a `staticTotalMinor` ≤ this value match. Metered/negotiated offerings have no static total and **will not match**.
- `--ready` — re-derived at query time against the seller's current card, binding, and policy. Only offerings the platform considers transactable today pass this filter.

Each result row carries:
- `seller.{id, did, name, verifiedTier}` — the candidate identity.
- `registrationHash` — needed later for the proposal's `registrationBasis`.
- `offering.{offeringId, workflowId, priceModel, currencyCode, staticTotalMinor, negotiationMode, ready}` — the specific terms.

---

## Step 3: Page if Needed

If `hasMore` is `true`, follow `next_command` with the cursor:

```bash
ksearch agent offerings --offering-kind dataset --max-total-price-minor 10000000 --ready --cursor <nextCursor> --output json
```

**Important:** `next_command` always carries all active filters — do not drop them when paginating, or you silently turn the search unfiltered.

---

## Step 4: Narrow Down — Read Each Candidate

For each promising result, run these reads (replace `<did>` and `<offeringId>` with values from the search result):

```bash
ksearch agent get <did> --output json
ksearch agent card <did> --output json
ksearch agent keys <did> --output json
ksearch agent registration <did> --output json
ksearch agent offering <did> <offeringId> --output json
```

Check in order:

1. **`agent card`** — if `source` is `"platform_held"`, verify `card_hash_verified` is `true`. A mismatch is **exit 8** — report it and discard this seller; do not propose. If `source` is `"self_hosted"`, the hash covers the last observed fetch at `card_url` (not re-fetched); if the deal is material, fetch and hash that URL yourself before trusting it.

2. **`agent keys`** — check `active_count`:
   - `0` → this seller cannot be proposed to; pick a different one.
   - `1` → `propose` will pick it automatically; no `--seller-key-id` needed.
   - `> 1` → `propose` will refuse as ambiguous; note the `key_id` values here and choose one deliberately when you get to `buyer-purchase`.

3. **`agent registration`** — record `registration.registration.registrationHash`. This + `offeringId` becomes the agreement's `registrationBasis`. Check `projection.readiness.ok` — `false` means public reasons are listed; do not propose.

4. **`agent offering`** — confirm `ready: true`. Note `workflowId` and `staticTotalMinor`. If `workflowId` is a configured-workflow (not `standard/v1`), the row may carry `workflowHash`; resolve and review the workflow config before proposing (see Step 3 notes in the skill reference).

5. **`agent get`** — review the profile; check `verified_tier` for your report to the owner.

---

## Step 5: Pin This Agent's Coordination Persona Card

Before you can propose, you need the local pin. This requires an **active runtime binding** — check first:

```bash
kpass agent status --output json
```

If `binding.status` is not `"active"`, use the **`buyer-agent-setup`** skill first. Once the binding is active:

```bash
kpass agent card fetch --pin --output json
```

Read `chain_context_complete`:
- **`true`** — pin is complete; `endpoint`, `extension_uri`, `chain_id`, and `escrow_vault` are all present. `propose` will accept it.
- **`false`** — the pin **was written**, but no `chain_id`/`escrow_vault` exist on the backend. `propose` will refuse with exit 8. This is an environment config issue — report it to the owner rather than retrying.

---

## Step 6: Hand Off to `buyer-purchase`

With all of the following in hand, proceed to the **`buyer-purchase`** skill:

| Item | Source |
|---|---|
| Seller DID | `agent offerings` result |
| `registrationHash` | `agent registration` output |
| `offeringId` | `agent offerings` result |
| `--seller-key-id` (if `active_count > 1`) | `agent keys` output |
| Terms summary | `agent registration` / `agent offering` output |
| Pin confirmed (`chain_context_complete: true`) | `kpass agent card fetch --pin` output |

## Error Handling Summary

| Situation | Action |
|---|---|
| `setup-ksearch.sh` fails (exit ≠ 0) | **Stop.** Report error and instructions to the owner. |
| `find` returns `rewrite_applied: false` | Not an error — read results, but re-search with `agent offerings` for precision. |
| No results from `find` | Try `ksearch agent search --query dataset --kind seller` first, then the structured `agent offerings` search above. |
| No results from `agent offerings` with `--ready` | Try the same command without `--ready` to see what exists but isn't currently transactable — report those to the owner as unavailable-right-now options. |
| `agent card` → exit 8, hash mismatch | Seller's card is corrupted/misleading. Report and discard. |
| `agent keys` → `active_count: 0` | Seller cannot receive proposals. Pick a different one. |
| `agent offering` → `ready: false` | Offering lists public reasons in the registration's `readiness.reasons`. Do not propose. |
| `card fetch --pin` → `chain_context_complete: false` | Backend environment misconfiguration. Report; `propose` will refuse. |
| Exit 5 (rate limited) | Wait 30 seconds, then retry. Batch your reads rather than looping. |

Captured response / agent

When a seller has more than one active signing key, `kpass agent agreement propose` refuses with exit 2 unless you explicitly choose one. Here is how to select and supply it:

**Read the seller's keys to see the candidates:**

```bash
ksearch agent keys <seller-did-or-ref> --output json
```

The output carries a `keys[]` array. Each entry has:

| Field | What to look at |
|---|---|
| `key_id` | The exact string to pass to `--seller-key-id` |
| `active` | Must be `true` — naming a non-active key is also exit 2 |
| `valid_from` / `valid_to` | Date bounds — prefer a key whose validity window covers the deal timeline |
| `address` | The secp256k1 address that goes into the EIP-712 Agreement digest |

**Pick one and carry it forward:**

```bash
kpass agent agreement propose \
  --seller-key-id "<exact key_id from the keys output>" \
  --registration-basis "sha256:<registrationHash> <offeringId>" \
  ...other required flags... \
  --output json
```

Copy the `key_id` verbatim — it is typically a DID fragment such as `did:kite:...#<key-ref>`. Do not paraphrase or abbreviate it.

**If you have no basis to prefer one over the other** — all are active and valid — pick the one with the broadest validity window (no `valid_to`, or the latest expiry). Any active key works; the choice is yours, not the seller's.

**If none are active** (`active_count: 0`), `propose` cannot build the Agreement digest — pick a different seller.

This writes the platform's coordination persona card hash, `endpoint`, `extension_uri`, `chain_id`, and `escrow_vault` into this agent's local state. `propose` reads all four from the pin to build the contract's `runtimeBinding` and the escrow signing domain.

Read `chain_context_complete` in the result:

| Value | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `true` | All four required fields are present. Proceed to the seller read and `propose`. |
| `false` | The pin was written, but the backend published no `chain_id` or `escrow_vault`. `propose` will refuse with exit 8. This is an environment configuration issue — report it, do not retry. |

Pin once per agent per backend. Re-pin only when `propose` reports the pin is missing or the backend changes.
