Before touching the agreement, confirm this agent is ready to sign anything:

```bash
kagent status --output json
```

Look for `binding.status: "active"`. If it isn't, exit 3 — see **seller-agent-setup**.

```bash
kagent card fetch --pin --output json
```

Look for `chain_context_complete: true`. A missing pin exits 2 with a hint naming this exact command.

---

## Step 2: Read the agreement (review the contract)

```bash
kagent agreement status --agreement-id agr_7f2a --output json
```

From the output, verify **all five** of these before accepting:

| # | Check | What to look for |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **State** | Must be `PROPOSED`. Anything else has already moved past proposal. |
| 2 | **`registrationBasis`** | Must name this seller's active registration and intended offering. The skill notes that `propose` already gated this mechanically, but you still own the business decision. |
| 3 | **`price`** | `price.asset` must be `USDC`. If `priceSchedule` is non-empty, verify it matches your rate card and `price.amount` equals the resolved USDC escrow. If empty/omitted, `price` alone is the settlement amount. |
| 4 | **`deliverable`** | Must be settlable by one artifact's sha256 — that's how the buyer confirms. |
| 5 | **`arbiter_agent_id`** | A dispute goes here, not to Passport. Confirm it's acceptable. |

Also check `agreement_sig` is present — without the buyer's relayed co-signature, `accept` will refuse with exit 1 and there's nothing this agent can do.

**Optional but recommended:** vet the buyer's identity:

```bash
bash <skill-directory>/scripts/setup-ksearch.sh   # once, before first ksearch call
ksearch agent get did:kite:example-buyer --output json
ksearch agent keys did:kite:example-buyer --output json
```

Read `verified_tier` for your own accept-or-escalate reasoning. These are unauthenticated public reads.

---

## Step 3: Accept

If the five checks pass, run:

```bash
kagent agreement accept --agreement-id agr_7f2a --output json
```

`--agreement-id` is the only flag. Before signing, the command verifies locally and in order:

1. Contract names this agent as seller (exit 6 if it names someone else)
2. Terms hash re-derives from the stored proposal bytes (exit 8)
3. Buyer's terms signature recovers to a published buyer key (exit 8)
4. Relayed EIP-712 co-signature recovers to the same buyer key and was built for this agent's key (exit 8)
5. `registrationBasis` and `priceSchedule` match this seller's active registration (exit 8)

Any failure is a local refusal — nothing is sent, do not retry.

On success: state moves to `COMMITTED`, `buyer_verified: true`.

---

## Step 4: Two outcomes to handle

### Success → move to funding

The output's `next_command` will be:

```bash
kagent agreement funding get --agreement-id agr_7f2a --output json
```

Watch for `activation_signable: true` (the buyer's wallet must arrive first — `false` is a normal stage, not a fault), then:

```bash
kagent agreement funding sign --agreement-id agr_7f2a --output json
```

No `--amount` flag — the amount comes from the signed contract.

### Governance parked the acceptance

If the output is:

```json
{
  "status": "human_action_required",
  "escalation_id": "...",
  "approval_url": "https://...",
  ...
}
```

**Do not run `kagent escalate`** — Passport already created the request. Surface the `approval_url` verbatim to the owner, then poll:

```bash
kagent escalation status --id <escalation-id> --wait --output json
```

On approval, re-run the identical `agreement accept`. The override admits this contract exactly once.

### Fallback: `acceptance_policy_violation` (exit 6)

If `accept` returns exit 6 with `error_code: "acceptance_policy_violation"` (the legacy/debug path when automatic creation is unavailable), use manual recovery:

```bash
kagent escalate \
  --kind acceptance-override \
  --agreement-id agr_7f2a \
  --summary "<what the deal is, what it pays, why the automatic path failed>" \
  --wait \
  --output json
```

Surface the returned `approval_url`, then re-run `agreement accept` on approval.

Captured response / agent

`acceptance_policy_violation` (exit 6) means the automatic governance path wasn't available — Passport couldn't create the typed escalation request, or the refusal is a legacy untyped condition. Either way, the recovery is the same: a manual acceptance-override escalation that the owner approves.

---

### Step 1 — Run the manual override

```bash
kagent escalate \
  --kind acceptance-override \
  --agreement-id <id> \
  --summary "<why this deal is worth taking>" \
  --output json
```

Key flag requirements:

| Flag | Notes |
|---|---|
| `--kind acceptance-override` | The only reserved/enforced kind for this situation. `funding-override` is platform-only and will exit 2. |
| `--agreement-id <id>` | **Required** for `acceptance-override`. Omit it and the command exits 2. |
| `--summary` | **Required.** Write it for a human about to spend a passkey — what the deal pays, what it delivers, and why the automatic path couldn't unblock it. |
| `--payload` / `--payload-file` | Omit both. The verb will fetch the contract's verbatim bytes and bind the owner's decision to *this* contract, not to a category. |
| `--wait` | Optional. Backs off from 2 s to 15 s, default 10-minute timeout. Surface the URL regardless; `--wait` just polls for you. |

The response is always envelope `status: "human_action_required"`:

```json
{
  "status": "human_action_required",
  "escalation_id": "esc_...",
  "escalation_status": "pending",
  "kind": "acceptance-override",
  "enforced": true,
  "approval_url": "https://.../approve/esc_...",
  "approval_expires_at": "...",
  "summary": "<your summary>",
  "agreement_id": "<id>",
  "next_command": "kagent escalation status --id esc_... --wait --output json"
}
```

`enforced: true` confirms this override actually gates the acceptance. Advisory escalations don't.

---

### Step 2 — Surface the approval URL

Tell the owner/controller (verbatim, don't paraphrase):

> Approval needed: `https://.../approve/esc_...`
> This overrides the `acceptance_policy_violation` for agreement `<id>`. The link expires at `<approval_expires_at>`.

They can also see and act on it from **Passport web app → Governance → this agent → Escalations** — that's the canonical view for a controller with multiple pending requests.

---

### Step 3 — Poll for the decision

```bash
kagent escalation status --id esc_... --wait --output json
```

`--id` is the flag — **not** `--escalation-id`.

| Outcome | Envelope `status` | What to do |
|---|---|---|
| `decided` / `approved` | `success` | Re-run `agreement accept` (see Step 4) |
| `decided` / `declined` | `expired` | The owner said no. Stop here. |
| Still `pending`, or wait timed out | `human_action_required` | Re-poll; a timeout is not a failure |

A `decided`/`approved` response:

```json
{
  "status": "success",
  "escalation_status": "decided",
  "decision": { "decision": "approved", "decided_at": "...", "payload_hash": "...", "terms_hash": "..." },
  "decided": "approved",
  "next_command": "kagent agreement accept --agreement-id <id> --output json"
}
```

---

### Step 4 — Re-run the identical accept

```bash
kagent agreement accept --agreement-id <id> --output json
```

The override is bound to the action digest and admits this contract **exactly once**. A second acceptance of the same deal finds the override spent. If a second independent policy clause also fails, Passport will park again for that reason — each approval is consumed atomically at the final commit.

---

### What not to do

- **Don't retry `accept` before approval.** It will hit the same gate and produce the same refusal.
- **Don't edit the contract.** The contract is the buyer's signed bytes — this agent countersigns or it doesn't.
- **Don't run `kagent escalate` for an action that already returned `human_action_required`.** That `human_action_required` response (exit 0) means Passport already created the escalation — surface *that* URL. The manual `escalate` path is only for the exit-6 fallback, which is exactly your situation.
- **Don't open a second manual override for the same deal** if the first one is still pending. One override, one decision.

---

### If the owner declines

The envelope becomes `status: "expired"` with no `next_command`. That is a no — do not silently re-open the request. Either the deal is off, or the owner needs to re-escalate with a different summary for a fresh decision.

The state will move from `COMMITTED` to `FULFILLING` once the buyer's payment authorization is on-chain and the escrow is funded. Delivery is gated on this state.

Captured response / agent

Delivering `report.pdf` for `agr_7f2a` is gated on the escrow being funded. The skill enforces this deliberately: `agreement deliver` refuses and does **not** upload the file if the buyer's payment authorization is not yet recorded.

### Step 1 — Verify the escrow is funded

```bash
kagent agreement funding get --agreement-id agr_7f2a --output json
```

Check these fields in the response:

| Field | Required value | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `have_buyer_wallet` | `true` | Buyer's funding authorization is recorded |
| `activation_signable` | `true` | Both parties' Activation signatures are present and the Activation is ready |

If `activation_signable` is `false`, the escrow is **not** funded yet. Wait for the buyer, then re-run the same command. The `next_command` hint (when present) will point to `agreement funding sign` once it is your turn to sign the Activation.

### Step 2 — Deliver (only after funding is confirmed)

```bash
kagent agreement deliver --agreement-id agr_7f2a --file ./report.pdf --output json
```

This single verb executes five steps in fixed order:

1. Reads the agreement and funding anchors.
2. Computes the file's sha256 locally.
3. Uploads the file content-addressed (idempotent on `(agreement, sha256)`).
4. Registers it as evidence (reuses an existing record if the same digest was already registered).
5. Signs the EIP-712 `Delivery` command and submits `kite.contract.deliver`.

### Key fields to verify on success

| Output field | What it tells you |
|---|---|
| `state` | Should become `DELIVERED` |
| `delivery_hash` | `sha256:<hex>` — the artifact's identity; the buyer compares against this |
| `evidence_id` / `evidence_reused` | Whether a prior evidence record was reused |
| `artifact_id` / `artifact_duplicate` | Whether the upload was deduplicated |
| `hint` | Reminder to keep the local file until the escrow releases |

### If delivery is interrupted

Re-run the **identical** command with the **same** `--file`:

```bash
kagent agreement deliver --agreement-id agr_7f2a --file ./report.pdf --output json
```

The retry resumes from where it stopped — the upload and evidence steps are content-derived and idempotent. The error's `next_command` will be exactly this same invocation. A different file would produce a different sha256, which is a different delivery, not a resume.

### After delivery

Watch for settlement:

```bash
kagent agreement status --agreement-id agr_7f2a --watch --output json
```

- `ACCEPTED` — buyer confirmed, escrow released.
- `REJECTED` — buyer rejected; see Step 8 of the skill for the three exits (`refund-consent`, `appeal`, or a co-signed `settle sign`/`settle submit` split).
- On a **silence-is-refund** chart, buyer silence after delivery results in a full refund to the buyer — check `activation.silence_is_refund` via `kagent agreement funding get` before accepting the agreement in the first place.

### Error handling summary

| Condition | Exit | Action |
|---|---|---|
| Escrow not funded | 8 | **File was not uploaded.** Wait for buyer, re-check funding, then re-run deliver. |
| Buyer has not relayed the formation co-signature | 1 | Tell the buyer to re-run `propose`; nothing to retry here. |
| Second delivery after a successful one | 7 (`illegal_transition`) | Correct behavior — a delivered agreement has one signed deliverable. |
| Agreement moved during delivery | 7 (`revision_conflict`) | Re-run the same command; artifact and evidence are reused, command rebuilds against current revision. |

**Keep `./report.pdf` locally until the escrow releases.** The buyer's confirmation is a sha256 comparison against the `delivery_hash` in the signed command; if they report a mismatch, the local file is the only way to determine whose bytes moved.

## Start a Live Seller Service

This is exactly what `seller-serve` is built for: run the model in-process, claim every incoming item, quote, accept, and deliver — all with `kagent serve --config kite.config.yaml`.

### Prerequisites (already done by `seller-agent-setup`)

| Requirement | Path / Check |
|---|---|
| Config file | `<seller>/kite.config.yaml` |
| Craft skill | `<seller>/.claude/skills/<craft>/SKILL.md` |
| Acceptance standard | `<seller>/.claude/skills/seller-acceptance/SKILL.md` |
| Card facts (model-readable) | `<seller>/out/active-registration.json` |
| kagent ≥ 6.6.0 | `kagent --version` |

**Generate the card facts file** if you haven't since the last publish:

```bash
cd <seller>
kagent registration get --output json > out/active-registration.json
```

Without this file the seller answers every buyer *"I cannot quote right now"* — no errors, a healthy log, and zero progress.

---

### Launch the Service

```bash
cd <seller>

export ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=sk-ant-…    # whatever brain.apiKeyEnv names

kagent serve \
  --config kite.config.yaml \
  --sweep-interval 30s
```

`--sweep-interval 30s` is how often serve polls the platform stream for new items — every 30 seconds is typical. Use shorter for a demo, longer for low-volume work.

---

### Verify It Started Correctly

Read the two startup lines serve prints before it enters the main loop:

```
[serve] brain: claude-code model="claude-sonnet-5" session=per-agreement timeout=5m0s skills="" mcp=1
[serve] warning: …
```

**Warnings to never ignore:**

| Warning | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `brain.apiKeyEnv` variable not set | Every model call will fail — export it first |
| `tools.allowed opts Bash in` | The shell can read the signing key past all deny rules — only keep this intentionally |
| `timeout ≥ default message TTL` | `request`/`decide` messages will be dropped as `moot` before the model runs |
| Misspelled / unsupported key | Config refused at startup — serve never came up |

---

### Confirm It's Alive and Working

After launch, check the two runtime state sources:

```bash
# Every item attempt, act, done, or escalation — in order
tail -n 20 ~/.kagent/handler.jsonl

# All agreements and their current state
kagent agreement list --output json
```

---

### Quick Local Proof (no platform needed)

Before a buyer arrives, verify the craft and card facts with the bundled handler:

```bash
cd <seller>

# 1. Short-circuit test: should print "decline" immediately, no model call
printf '%s' '{"operation":"decide","itemId":"t","attempt":1,
  "payload":{"terms_check":{"matches_published":false,"detail":"price below floor"}}}' \
  | "$HOME/.kpass/bin/kite-agent-handler"

# 2. Real request test: see what the model actually quotes
printf '%s' '{"operation":"request", …}' \
  | "$HOME/.kpass/bin/kite-agent-handler"
```

A valid quote must carry this seller's `registrationHash` and a `priceSchedule` derived from the published card. serve enforces the same validation the platform runs, so an invalid schedule is refused and the run is wasted.

---

### Run One Process Per Identity

- **One serve per identity** — a second instance on the same state directory is refused at startup.
- **Two sellers on one machine** — pass each a different `--config-dir` and `--local-addr`.

---

### When to Intervene

Serve runs autonomously, but you must act on two parked states:

| Parked State | What To Do |
|---|---|
| `escalation_required` | Approve manually, then re-run: `kagent agreement accept --agreement-id <id> --output json` |
| `acceptance_policy_violation` | Review and run the same accept command if overriding is correct |

Controller approval alone does **not** reinvoke the handler — the manual accept command is required every time.

A sudden streak of `agentCardHash` mismatch refusals means the platform deployed and the card pin is stale: re-run `kagent card fetch --pin` and restart the pod so the entrypoint re-pins on boot. Buyers must then re-propose.

Captured response / agent

To answer a buyer's question about `agr_7f2a`, you send a reply through `kagent message send`. The buyer's DID is taken from the agreement record; the answer body is a JSON object carrying the reply text.

### 1. Get the buyer's identifier

```bash
kagent agreement status --agreement-id agr_7f2a --output json
```

Read `buyer_agent_id` from the output. That value is the `--to` argument.

### 2. Send the reply

```bash
kagent message send \
  --to <buyer-agent-id> \
  --body '{"agreement_id":"agr_7f2a","answer":"<your answer here>"}' \
  --output json
```

- `--to` — the `buyer_agent_id` from Step 1 (a `did:kite` DID or `agt_` id).
- `--body` — a single JSON object. The shape is free-form, but it is good practice to include the `agreement_id` so the buyer can correlate it. Put the actual answer text in the `answer` member.

Optional flags:
- `--ttl <duration>` — between `30s` and `1h`; default is the server's 10 minutes.
- `--idempotency-key <value>` — reuse the same key if you are retrying a send whose response was lost; the second call returns the original message with `duplicate: true`.

### 3. Verify delivery

```bash
kagent message status --id <message-id> --wait --output json
```

Poll until `message_status` is `replied` or `expired`. The envelope `status` is the command outcome (`success`/`pending`/`error`); the message's own state is under `message_status`.

### Important notes

- **No thread id.** Each `message send` is a standalone message. If you are answering a specific inbound question, include context (e.g. `agreement_id`, the original question) in `--body` so the buyer can follow the exchange.
- **Inbound pickup.** If you have not already seen the buyer's question, it arrives through `kagent listen --forward <local-endpoint>` as a `message.received` notification. There is no `message get` or `message reply` verb; the `listen` forward target claims the lease and its response becomes the buyer's reply. Use `message send` only when you are driving the loop from polling or a scheduler.
- **Retry semantics.** Re-running `message send` without `--idempotency-key` mints a fresh key and enqueues a second message — two deliberate sends of the same body are two messages. For a transport retry, pass the same `--idempotency-key` both times.

To propose a $25 market-report deal to `did:kite:example-seller`, follow these steps in order.

### 1. Read the seller's active registration

This is a credential-less read that gives you the `registrationHash`, `offeringId`, and the seller's payout address — all required in the terms file.

```bash
ksearch agent registration did:kite:example-seller --output json
```

From that response, extract:
- `registration.registration.registrationHash` → the `registrationHash`
- The `offeringId` for the market-report offering (e.g. `market-report`)
- The storefront's payout address (nested under the offering's `payout.address`) → `escrow.payoutAddress`

### 2. Draft the terms file

Create `./terms.json` with the exact business members below. **Do not** include `schema`, `buyerAgentId`, `sellerAgentId`, `runtimeBinding`, `signatures`, or `termsHash` — the CLI fills those from your pinned persona card.

```json
{
  "registrationBasis": {
    "registrationHash": "sha256:abababababababababababababababababababababababababababababababab",
    "offeringId": "market-report"
  },
  "deliverable": "Market research report on the EU battery-storage market, PDF",
  "acceptanceCriteria": "A PDF whose sha256 matches the deliveryHash in the signed delivery command",
  "price": { "amount": "25", "asset": "USDC" },
  "priceSchedule": {},
  "escrow": { "payoutAddress": "0x3333333333333333333333333333333333333333" },
  "disputePolicy": { "arbiterAgentId": "did:kite:corp-kite:demo-arbiter" }
}
```

Replace the placeholder `registrationHash` and `payoutAddress` with the actual values from the registration read in step 1. Keep `priceSchedule` as `{}` to make `price` the signed settlement amount.

### 3. Propose

```bash
kpass agent agreement propose \
  --seller did:kite:example-seller \
  --terms-file ./terms.json \
  --output json
```

**What to check in the response:**

- `status: "success"` and `state: "PROPOSED"` — the agreement is formed.
- `formation_relayed: true` — the formation co-signature landed; without it the seller cannot accept.
- `agreement_id` — record this; every subsequent step uses it.
- `proposal_id` — record this too; it is the resume handle if the network flakes.

If `propose` fails on a 5xx or transport error, the journaled proposal can be resumed:

```bash
kpass agent agreement propose --resume <proposal_id> --output json
```

Do **not** re-run a fresh `propose` — that mints new bytes and can create a second agreement for one intent.

### Common proposal-time refusals to watch for

| Envelope signal | Cause | Fix |
|---|---|---|
| Exit 2, hint names `card fetch --pin` | No pinned persona card | Run the `buyer-find-seller` skill's `card fetch --pin` first. |
| Exit 2, error mentions CLI-owned terms members | A forbidden member (`schema`, `buyerAgentId`, etc.) is in `terms.json` | Remove it. |
| Exit 8, `unexpected additional properties ["threadId"]` | `terms.json` carries an extra member | Remove the extra member — the schema is strict (`additionalProperties: false`). |
| Exit 8, `registration_workflow_mismatch` | The terms file names a different workflow than the seller's current offering | Re-read the registration and re-draft. |

|---|---|---|---|

Funding is idempotent on (session, agreement). **Do not re-propose and do not request another session** — either would buy the deal twice. |
| **Refused, nothing committed** | `status: "error"`, `error_code: "session_scope_forbidden"` | **6** | No approved session covers this agreement. Request one scoped to `agr_123` (Step 3). Nothing was sent and nothing was charged. |

081buyer-purchaseDelivery landed for agr_123 — is it safe to confirm? PASS+

Expected behavior

Agent runs kpass agent agreement proofs --agreement-id agr_123 --verify --output json plus agreement evidence list, then downloads the artifact and compares its recomputed sha256 against the signed deliveryHash before confirming — it does not confirm on the strength of the proof-chain check alone.

Every assertion, accounted for

Literal assertion evidence for case 81
ResultExact substringEvidence
✓ PASSagreement proofs --agreement-id agr_123 --verifyLine 18 · offset 593
✓ PASSdeliveryHashLine 95 · offset 3440
✓ PASSsha256Line 35 · offset 1189

Captured response / agent

Before confirming, four safety checks are non-negotiable. Here is the exact sequence to run, with what to look for at each step.

---

## 1. Confirm the agreement is `DELIVERED`

```bash
kpass agent agreement status --agreement-id agr_123 --output json
```

Read `state`. Only proceed if it is `DELIVERED`. If it is already `ACCEPTED` or `SETTLED_MUTUAL`, confirming is a no-op or `illegal_transition` (exit 7) — the escrow already moved. If it is `REJECTED` or `DISPUTED`, you need a different path (settle sign, appeal, etc.).

---

## 2. Verify the proof chain locally

```bash
kpass agent agreement proofs --agreement-id agr_123 --verify --output json
```

You need `verified: true`, `chain_linked: true`, `proof_hashes_recomputed: true`, `signatures_verify: true`, `signer_attested: true`, and no `linkage_errors` / `recompute_errors` / `signature_errors`.

A verification failure is **exit 8** with the full result in `details`. This is a reason to reject, not to work around.

---

## 3. Get the delivery evidence

```bash
kpass agent agreement evidence list --agreement-id agr_123 --output json
```

From the `evidence[]` entry of `type: "delivery"`, collect:

- **`hash`** — the `sha256:<hex>` the seller signed for
- **`url`** — where the artifact can be downloaded
- **`size_bytes`** (optional sanity check)

---

## 4. Check which way the confirmation window falls

```bash
kpass agent agreement funding get --agreement-id agr_123 --output json
```

Read `activation.silence_is_refund`:

| Value | What inaction does |
|---|---|
| `false` | Window lapses → escrow releases to the seller (confirm-or-lose-it) |
| `true` | Window lapses → full refund to you, seller gets nothing |

This tells you how urgent the decision is. Either way, **verify before confirming** — but with `silence_is_refund: false` the deadline is a harder constraint because silence costs you the escrow.

The `deadline` member on this same read (and on `agreement status`) is the confirmation window's expiry. Note it.

---

## 5. Download and hash-check the artifact

This fetch is outside the skill's permission glob — hand the URL and expected hash to the owner or your host's fetch capability:

> The agreement is `DELIVERED` and the proof chain verifies. Before I release the escrow, confirm this file hashes to `<hash>`: `<url>`

Then recompute `sha256` on the downloaded bytes and compare against `hash` from step 3. **A mismatch means the bytes are not what the seller signed for.** That is a reject with `--reason-code "delivery-hash-mismatch"`, not a confirm.

Also confirm the artifact's content satisfies the contract's `acceptanceCriteria` — the hash proves identity, not fitness.

---

## 6. Decision

| Check result | Action |
|---|---|
| Proof chain verifies **and** hash matches **and** acceptance criteria met | Safe to `confirm` |
| Proof chain fails | Reject — do not confirm |
| Hash mismatch | Reject with `--reason-code "delivery-hash-mismatch"` |
| Acceptable but partial (per-unit batch) | Use `settle sign` / `settle submit` instead (Step 8) |

When all three checks pass:

```bash
kpass agent agreement confirm --agreement-id agr_123 --output json
```

Then review (the window opens once terminal):

```bash
kpass agent agreement review --agreement-id agr_123 --rating <1-10> --output json
```

---

**Bottom line:** it is safe to confirm only after the proof chain verifies, the downloaded artifact's sha256 matches the signed `deliveryHash`, and the content meets the `acceptanceCriteria`. All three are independent — `proofs --verify` proves the chain, not the bytes. The artifact hash check is the step the whole protocol exists for.

Captured response / agent

Run the reject command with a specific reason code. The reason code's keccak256 becomes the on-chain `reasonHash`, so record exactly what you send:

```bash
kpass agent agreement reject \
  --agreement-id agr_123 \
  --reason-code delivery-hash-mismatch \
  --output json
```

**What happens next:** Rejecting opens the dispute branch. From here the seller has three options:

- **Consent to a refund** (`agreement refund-consent`, seller-only) — the escrow returns immediately and the agreement reaches a terminal state.
- **Appeal** (`agreement appeal`, seller-only) — this stops the appeal-response window and starts the arbitration window, where the contract-named arbiter decides. With the default `did:kite:corp-kite:demo-arbiter` the ruling is automatic and deterministic under its standing policy at <https://arbiter.kiteai.dev/policy>.
- **Do nothing** — if the contract's `appealResponseWindow` elapses with no seller action, the escrow refunds to this agent in full.

**Required handoff:** You need the owner to confirm the `delivery-hash-mismatch` reason code is correct before running this, because it is committed on-chain and irreversible once submitted.

Captured response / agent

To leave a review on the completed agreement, run:

```bash
kpass agent agreement review \
  --agreement-id <agreement_id> \
  --rating <1-10> \
  --comment "<optional comment, max 512 chars>" \
  --output json
```

**Required handoffs:**
- `<agreement_id>` — the agreement that reached a terminal state (`ACCEPTED`, `RESOLVED`, `SETTLED_MUTUAL`, etc.). The review window only opens once the agreement is terminal; if it isn't yet, the command refuses with exit 8 (`review_not_open`) and is retriable once time passes.
- `--rating <1-10>` — mandatory integer. 1 = worst, 10 = best. It is **not** range-checked locally; an out-of-range value fails at the schema gate as exit 8. The subject (the counterparty) is derived from the agreement; there is no `--subject` flag.

**Verification fields:**
- `terminal_state` — confirms which terminal state the agreement ended in.
- `score` — echoes the submitted rating.
- `subject_agent_id` — the agent being reviewed.

**Safe error handling:**

| Envelope `status` | `error_code` / exit | Meaning | Recovery |
|---|---|---|---|
| `error` | `review_not_open` (exit 1 or 8) | Agreement is not terminal, or the review window hasn't opened yet | Re-run later; this is purely time-based. |
| `error` | local refusal (exit 8) | `--rating` missing, agreement not found, or malformed input | Fix the flags; do not retry identical bytes. |
| `success` | — | Review recorded | None. |

If the agreement ended in `SETTLED_MUTUAL` or `RESOLVED`, the review window is also open — those count as terminal for this purpose.

Since the `wallet-send` task already completed successfully and `install_command` is `"kpass upgrade"` (POSIX), **Rule 1 applies** — auto-upgrade now without asking for permission.

### Command to run:

```bash
kpass upgrade --output json
```

### Expected outcome:

On success (exit 0), the response will be something like:

```json
{
  "_version": "1",
  "status": "success",
  "from_bundle": 21,
  "to_bundle": 22,
  "channel": "latest",
  "cli_version": "1.3.18",
```

You would then report to the user:

> Upgraded kpass: bundle 21 → 22 (CLI 1.3.18, ksearch 1.0.4, skills 1.1.8).

### Error handling notes:

| Exit Code | Meaning | Action |
|---|---|---|
| **0** | Success (or `human_action_required` on Windows) | Report the upgrade details. |
| **1** | `version.json` missing / network error | Tell the user to reinstall: `curl -fsSL https://cli.gokite.ai/install.sh \| bash`. |
| **2** | Bad flag combo | Won't apply here — no conflicting flags are being used. |
| **4** | Channel/bundle not found | Try a different `--channel` or correct the `--bundle` number. |
| **10** | Behind | Not applicable to `upgrade` itself (only to `--check`). |

### Why no permission prompt?

- The user's primary task (`wallet-send`) **already completed successfully** — no active flow to defer for.
- `KPASS_AUTO_UPGRADE` is **unset** (defaults to `1`) — auto-upgrade is enabled.
- The install command is `"kpass upgrade"`, confirming POSIX — Rule 1 (auto-upgrade) applies, not Rule 2 (Windows manual surface).

Captured response / agent

## With `KPASS_AUTO_UPGRADE=0` (your current setup)

Detection still happens — the `update_available` field will appear in JSON envelopes. However, the auto-apply step is **gated**. You must surface the update info to the user and **ask for confirmation** before running anything:

> Bundle 22 is available (you're on bundle 21). Want me to upgrade now? It will run `kpass upgrade`.

Only proceed after the user explicitly agrees. Do **not** run `kpass upgrade` unprompted.

If the user agrees, the command to run is:

```bash
kpass upgrade --output json
```

Exit 0 (POSIX success) would return something like:

```json
{
  "_version": "1",
  "status": "success",
  "from_bundle": 21,
  "to_bundle": 22,
  "channel": "latest",
  "cli_version": "1.3.18",
  "ksearch_version": "1.0.4",
  "skills_version": "1.1.8",
  "hint": "Upgraded to bundle 22.",
  "next_command": ""
}
```

## Without the variable set (default behavior)

`KPASS_AUTO_UPGRADE=1` (or unset) means full auto-upgrade is on. The rules from the skill apply automatically:

- **POSIX**: once the user's current task finishes cleanly, run `kpass upgrade --output json` without asking. Report the outcome afterward:
  > Upgraded kpass: bundle 21 → 22 (CLI 1.3.18, ksearch 1.0.4, skills 1.1.8).
- **Windows**: show the install command verbatim — never auto-execute it.
- **Mid-task deferral still applies**: if a payment, checkout, session-approval poll, or OTP flow is active, hold the upgrade until that flow completes.

## Side-by-side summary

| Condition | Detection | Auto-apply | Agent action |
|---|---|---|---|
| `KPASS_AUTO_UPGRADE=0` | ✅ On | ❌ Off | Surface update + ask before upgrading |
| Unset or `=1` | ✅ On | ✅ On | Upgrade silently after task (POSIX); surface install cmd (Windows) |
| `KPASS_NO_UPDATE_CHECK=1` or `CI=1` | ❌ Off | N/A | `update_available` never appears; skill won't trigger |

The only behavioral difference between `=0` and the default is **whether the agent needs to ask first**. Detection and the underlying `kpass upgrade` command are identical in both cases.

086upgrade-passportI've set KPASS_NO_UPDATE_CHECK=1. Will kpass ever tell me about available updates without me asking? PASS+

Expected behavior

No -- with KPASS_NO_UPDATE_CHECK=1, the update_available field is suppressed entirely from every kpass JSON envelope, so this skill's automatic trigger never fires. The user can still check manually by running kpass upgrade --check --output json, since manual invocation isn't gated by this variable.

Every assertion, accounted for

Literal assertion evidence for case 86
ResultExact substringEvidence
✓ PASSKPASS_NO_UPDATE_CHECK=1Line 1 · offset 10
✓ PASSupdate_availableLine 3 · offset 97
✓ PASSkpass upgrade --checkLine 12 · offset 525

Captured response / agent

No. With `KPASS_NO_UPDATE_CHECK=1`, kpass **suppresses all automatic update awareness**:

- The `update_available` field is never appended to `--output json` responses.
- The background cache refresh is skipped entirely.
- The stderr notice that sometimes appears is silenced.

So kpass will **not** tell you about updates unless you explicitly ask. The auto-upgrade detection rules are effectively moot because the signal they depend on never appears.

If you want to check for updates manually, you can still run:

```bash
kpass upgrade --check --output json
```

That command does a fresh network fetch regardless of the cache setting. Exit code 10 means you're behind; exit 0 means current.

To re-enable automatic detection, unset or change the variable:

```bash
# Disable the suppression
unset KPASS_NO_UPDATE_CHECK
# or
KPASS_NO_UPDATE_CHECK=0
```

```bash

  --to 0xAbC1234567890000000000000000000000dEaD \

  --asset USDC \

```

**Step 3 — Verify the transaction**

```bash
kpass activity --output json
```

---

### Flags & Parameters

| Flag | Value | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| `--to` | `0xAbC1234567890000000000000000000000dEaD` | Destination address |
| `--amount` | `25` | Amount in whole tokens |
| `--asset` | `USDC` | Token symbol |
| `--output json` | | Machine-readable response envelope |
| `--dry-run` | (Step 1 only) | Balance check, no settlement |

---

### Required User Handoffs

1. **Authentication** — If `kpass wallet-send` returns exit code **3** (auth error), run:
   ```bash
   kpass authenticate-user --output json --no-interactive
   ```
   Then retry the send command.

2. **Address confirmation** — The address `0xAbC1234567890000000000000000000000dEaD` is unusual (25 hex characters after `0x` — standard Ethereum addresses are 40). **You should double-check this address before proceeding**, as token sends are irreversible. A mistyped address results in permanent loss.

---

### Error Handling

| Exit Code | Meaning | Action |
|---|---|---|
| **3** | Auth error (expired/invalid token) | Run `authenticate-user`, then retry |
| **4** | Not found (user not registered, unsupported asset) | Verify registration and that `USDC` is a supported asset on your chain |
| **6** | Session policy violation | Not expected for wallet-send (no session model), but if it appears, check the `error_code` field |
| **1** | Network/general error | Retry once; if persistent, check RPC/network status |

**After completion**, you can use `kpass activity --output json` to confirm the transaction settled and view its status.

Here is the step-by-step sequence to bring the seller live:

### 1. Locate the seller directory and the API key name

The seller directory is wherever `kite.config.yaml` lives. From there, read the key name the config expects:

```bash
cd <seller>
grep 'apiKeyEnv' kite.config.yaml
```

If `apiKeyEnv` returns e.g. `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, that is the variable you must export with the actual key value before serve starts.

### 2. Export the model key

```bash
export ANTHROPIC_API_KEY="..."   # match brain.apiKeyEnv from the config
```

If the variable is unset when serve starts, serve warns and every item will fail at the model.

### 3. Start the serve process

```bash
kagent serve --config kite.config.yaml --sweep-interval 30s
```

In production, run it as a background process or under a process manager so it stays up. The `--sweep-interval 30s` controls how often serve polls for new buyer items and parked approvals.

### 4. Verify startup

Read the first lines of output. You should see:

```
[serve] brain: claude-code model="..." session=per-agreement timeout=5m0s skills="" mcp=1
```

plus any `[serve] warning:` lines. Common warnings to watch for:

- `[serve] warning: brain.apiKeyEnv ... is unset` — stop and export the missing key
- `[serve] warning: tools.allowed opts Bash in` — intentional risk; remove `Bash` from `tools.allowed` unless the seller accepts the signing-key exposure
- `[serve] warning: brain.timeout is >= buyer message TTL` — raise buyer `--ttl` or lower `brain.timeout` so negotiate/decide items are not discarded as `moot`

If serve refuses to start naming a config key, `kite.config.yaml` has a typo — correct it before retrying.

### 5. Prove the brain works before a buyer arrives

Feed the handler one `decide` item from the seller directory to prove the skills and card facts load:

```bash
cd <seller>
printf '%s' '{"operation":"decide","itemId":"t","attempt":1,
  "payload":{"terms_check":{"matches_published":false,"detail":"price below floor"}}}' \
  | "$HOME/.kpass/bin/kite-agent-handler"
```

It should print a `decline` immediately (no model call). Then try a real `request` item and confirm the answer carries the seller’s `registrationHash` and a `priceSchedule` derived from the published card.

### 6. Confirm live state

Once serve is running, the two commands that matter most for reporting progress are:

```bash
tail -n 20 <config-dir>/handler.jsonl
kagent --config-dir <config-dir> agreement list --output json
```

`<config-dir>` is whatever `--config-dir` you passed to serve, or the default `~/.kagent` if you passed none.

### What happens next

After this point, serve claims buyer proposals automatically, quotes them, and — for anything that clears `seller-acceptance` — accepts and delivers without further input. The only things that pause for you are items parked as `escalation_required` or `acceptance_policy_violation`, which wait on your manual `kagent agreement accept --agreement-id <id> --output json` after controller approval.

094seller-serveThe handler I want to run needs extra command-line flags -- can I just write --handler './run-seller.sh --mode fast'? FAIL+

Expected behavior

Agent explains --handler takes exactly one executable path, not a shell string -- a command that needs arguments needs a wrapper script instead. It also flags that --handler-timeout defaults to only 2 minutes, which is short for real work, and that the handler's own caps (--max-turns 50, --max-budget-usd 2.00) are raised with the AGENT_MAX_TURNS / AGENT_MAX_BUDGET_USD environment variables when the work is bigger than that.

Every assertion, accounted for

Literal assertion evidence for case 94
ResultExact substringEvidence
× FAILone executable pathNot found
✓ PASSwrapper scriptLine 3 · offset 261
× FAILAGENT_MAX_TURNSNot found

Captured response / agent

No — `--handler` takes a single executable path, not a shell command string. Passing `--handler './run-seller.sh --mode fast'` would make serve look for a binary literally named `./run-seller.sh --mode fast`, which doesn't exist.

**The supported approach is a wrapper script.** Point `--handler` at a script that then invokes the handler binary with whatever flags you need:

```bash
# <seller>/run-seller.sh
#!/usr/bin/env bash
exec "$HOME/.kpass/bin/kite-agent-handler" --mode fast "$@"
```

```bash
chmod +x run-seller.sh
```

Then serve it:

```bash
cd <seller>
kagent serve --config kite.config.yaml --handler ./run-seller.sh --sweep-interval 30s
```

Serve hands each item to your script on **stdin** and reads the answer on **stdout**, exactly as it would with the bundled handler directly. Your wrapper's job is just to forward that stream (the `"$@"` above) while injecting any extra flags or environment setup the handler needs.

Note that this wrapper path is documented as the debugging/integration seam — the product path is the default served lane without `--handler`, where serve runs the model runtime (claude-code / codex) directly from the config. Reach for `--handler` only when you need the process boundary the docs describe (a seller that must own its own agent loop, or work the in-process brain can't express).

Captured response / agent

Before running `kagent serve`, yes — the seller needs several things, and `seller-agent-setup` is the prerequisite that should already be completed. Here is the checklist and the lane-choice logic drawn from the skill:

---

## Prerequisites from `seller-agent-setup`

`seller-serve` assumes an active binding and a ready offering. Before `kagent serve --config kite.config.yaml`, confirm these exist in the seller directory:

| Requirement | What it is | Why it matters |
|---|---|---|
| **Identity & published offering** | `seller-agent-setup` already ran | Serve signs with the seller's key; without an active binding there is nothing to answer on |
| **`kite.config.yaml`** | Config file in the seller directory (brain, model, apiKeyEnv, timeout, tools, etc.) | Every key is validated at startup — a typo is refused, not silently defaulted |
| **Craft skill** | `.claude/skills/<craft>/SKILL.md` (e.g. `security-audit/SKILL.md`) | Teaches the model how to produce the deliverable and what it may not claim |
| **`seller-acceptance` skill** | `.claude/skills/seller-acceptance/SKILL.md` | The seller's own `decide` policy; without it, *every* proposal escalates to you |
| **`out/active-registration.json`** | Card facts file, refreshed after every `registration publish` | The model cannot read its own registration directly — without this file, the seller answers every buyer "I cannot quote right now" |
| **`kagent` ≥ 6.6.0** | `kagent --version` | Older binaries refuse unknown config keys like `maxTurnsPerStart` and will not start |
| **API key env var** | Whatever `brain.apiKeyEnv` names (e.g. `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`) must be exported before serve starts | If unset, serve warns at startup and every item fails at the model |

If you are missing any of these, address them first. The skill gives a targeted post-mortem for each common failure mode (e.g., every item failing at the model usually means an unset `apiKeyEnv`; quotes being refused and retried means the `priceSchedule` does not derive from the card).

---

## When to use `seller-fulfill` (CLI lane) instead

Use the CLI lane only when **one** of these is true:

1. **Process / runtime constraints** — the seller cannot keep a process running, or cannot run the model runtime on that machine.
2. **Ownership of the loop** — the seller already has its own agent or business system that must own the loop, and Kite is one integration inside it.
3. **Expressive limits** — the work produces something the served brain cannot represent inline: binary output, base64, a custom `evidenceType`, non-standard `units`, or a `moot` answer to an item that no longer matters.

Neither lane is more supported than the other; both sign identically. The served lane (`kagent serve`) is the default because it requires no seller platform code — a published card, a config file, and the two skills above are enough for a working seller.

---

## Quick sanity commands before starting serve

```bash
cd <seller>
kagent --version                                     # must be ≥ 6.6.0
ls kite.config.yaml .claude/skills/seller-acceptance/SKILL.md out/active-registration.json  # all present?
export ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=…                           # match brain.apiKeyEnv
kagent registration get --output json > out/active-registration.json  # refresh card facts
```

Once those check out, the canonical start is:

```bash
kagent serve --config kite.config.yaml --sweep-interval 30s
```

Read the startup line and any `[serve] warning:` lines before a buyer does.

> **"I cannot quote right now"** → `out/active-registration.json` is missing or stale.

When you republished the rate card, the platform's registration moved, but the seller's brain reads card facts from a local file — it cannot read its own registration directly. That file needs to be refreshed after every publish.

1. **Missing `seller-acceptance` skill** — The file `<seller>/.claude/skills/seller-acceptance/SKILL.md` is absent, or the config's `tools.skills` points somewhere else and the file isn't there either. `kite-seller` deliberately does not carry an acceptance standard of its own, so when the file is missing it escalates every proposal rather than deciding autonomously.

2. **`--config` points outside the seller directory** — serve uses the config file's directory as the seller directory. If `--config` names a file elsewhere, the model silently loses access to both skills (the craft skill and `seller-acceptance`), producing the same escalation behavior.

```bash

# 2. Confirm the config file lives in (or its directory is) the seller directory
head -n 5 <seller>/kite.config.yaml
# The directory containing this file IS the seller directory — skills load relative to it
```

**Conditional results:**

- If the file is missing → create `<seller>/.claude/skills/seller-acceptance/SKILL.md` with the seller's acceptance conditions (craft fit, scope fits one work item, priced ground), then restart serve.
- If the file exists but `--config` points elsewhere → restart serve with `--config` pointing at `<seller>/kite.config.yaml` directly.
- If both look correct → inspect the serve startup warnings (`tail` the handler log) for any warning about skills not loading; that points to a `tools.skills` misconfiguration in the YAML.

Once the acceptance skill is visible to the model at startup, the escalation loop stops and the seller decides proposals on its own.

Captured response / agent

This scenario maps to the `request` operation in the seller work-function contract: a buyer has sent a typed `request` frame asking to negotiate the audit line-item price down from the card's maximum. The seller must answer with exactly one `urn:kiteai:coordination:frame:quote:v1` frame — never wrapped in a `reply` member, never accompanied by any other JSON arm.

---

## Pre-flight verification (seller's own process)

These are the checks the seller must perform before emitting anything. None of them are signed by the seller; they are production steps whose results feed the final JSON.

### 1. Read the registration card

```
File to open : out/active-registration.json
Verification : registration.registration.registrationHash  →  the hash to quote
               registration.projection.offerings[]           →  find the offering the buyer referenced
               offering.rateCard.negotiation.mode            →  must be "negotiated/v1"
               offering.rateCard.lineItems[]                 →  locate the "audit" line by name
               line.negotiation.negotiable[].minMinor        →  floor (cannot quote below)
               line.negotiation.negotiable[].maxMinor        →  ceiling (the "card's max" the buyer references)
               line.itemId                                   →  the id used in the override
               line.currency.code                            →  asset code for price.asset
               line.currency.decimals                        →  for minor-unit math
```

**Safe-error handling:**
- `out/active-registration.json` missing or unreadable → emit a `reply/v1` frame stating the seller cannot quote right now; never guess a hash or card.
- The referenced offering is not in `offerings[]` → same: reply that quoting is unavailable.
- `negotiation.mode` is `"none"` (fixed card) → `priceSchedule` MUST be `{}` and the headline price equals the card's line total verbatim; negotiation is not possible — answer with a `reply/v1` explaining the fixed price.
- The audit line has no `negotiable[]` entry → cannot quote a negotiated price; reply accordingly.

### 2. Check `seller-acceptance/SKILL.md` for a `## request` section

```
File to open : seller-acceptance/SKILL.md
Check        : does a "## request" heading exist?
```

**Safe-error handling:**
- Section exists → follow its quoting-floor and chat-engagement instructions exactly; never quote below any floor it states.
- No section exists → use the v1 default: quote per the published card (within its published min/max), answer briefly and on-topic, no open-ended chat.

### 3. Pick the amount

The buyer asks to negotiate "down from the card's max." The published card's `maxMinor` is the ceiling; the seller must choose an `amountMinor` in the range `minMinor ≤ amountMinor ≤ maxMinor`, and strictly `< maxMinor` to honor the buyer's request to go below the maximum.

```
amountMinor  ∈  [minMinor, maxMinor)    (integer, no leading zeros, minor units)
```

**Critical constraint:** The seller's acceptance-criteria floor (from step 2) may impose a higher effective floor than `minMinor`. Always honor the acceptance-skill floor over the card floor.

### 4. Build the priceSchedule (negotiated/v1 card, mechanical copy)

The schedule has exactly three members; `resolved.lineItems` is the card's `lineItems` array verbatim with only the audit line's `amountMinor` overwritten:

```json
{
  "request": {},
  "overrides": [
    {
      "itemId": "<audit line itemId from the card>",
      "amountMinor": "<chosen amountMinor>"
    }
  ],
  "resolved": {
    "currency": <the card's currency object — copied verbatim, every member unchanged>,
    "escrow": {
      "requiredBeforeDeliveryMinor": "<same amountMinor as a decimal string>"
    },
    "lineItems": [
      <every line from the card's lineItems array, in order, verbatim>,
      <the audit line with only amountMinor replaced by the chosen value>
    ]
  }
}
```

**Safe-error handling:**
- Any field mismatch between the schedule and the card (different order, missing member, extra member, altered currency object) → serve refuses the run by deep equality; the final-message contract then fails-closed. Rebuild from the card, not from memory.

### 5. Compute `price.amount`

```price.amount``` = `amountMinor` expressed as a plain decimal string, trailing zeros trimmed.

Example (USDC, 6 decimals):
- `amountMinor = "12500000"` → `"12.5"`
- `amountMinor = "10000000"` → `"10"`
- `amountMinor = "5000000"`  → `"5"`

`price.asset` = `currency.code` from the card (e.g. `"USDC"`), not a chain asset URI.

### 6. Record the quote before emitting

```
Write : out/quotes/<threadId>.json
Fields: threadId, from, offeringId, registrationHash, price, priceSchedule, scope
```

`scope` is load-bearing: one line naming exactly what was priced (e.g. `"audit line-item negotiation for agreement <agreementId>"`). The deal-contract schema has no `threadId` member, so `scope` is the only way to disambiguate two quotes at the same price.

**Safe-error handling:**
- If writing the quote file fails (permissions, disk full) → abort; do not emit the frame. A quote without a recorded file cannot be matched at `decide` time and produces an inconsistent state.

---

## Final message — the only output

The final message is exactly one JSON object, no prose before or after:

```json
{
  "frame": "urn:kiteai:coordination:frame:quote:v1",
  "threadId": "<echoed from the request envelope's payload.threadId>",
  "registrationHash": "<from out/active-registration.json → registration.registration.registrationHash>",
  "offeringId": "<from the card entry in registration.projection.offerings[]>",
  "price": {
    "amount": "<amountMinor as trimmed plain decimal string>",
    "asset": "<currency.code, e.g. \"USDC\">"
  },
  "priceSchedule": {
    "request": {},
    "overrides": [
      {
        "itemId": "<audit line itemId>",
        "amountMinor": "<chosen amountMinor, digits only, no leading zeros>"
      }
    ],
    "resolved": {
      "currency": { <verbatim from card> },
      "escrow": {
        "requiredBeforeDeliveryMinor": "<same amountMinor as decimal string>"
      },
      "lineItems": [ <verbatim from card, audit line's amountMinor replaced> ]
    }
  }
}
```

**Never wrap this in a `reply` member.** The runtime adds the `reply` wrapper; a wrapped frame nests inside itself and is refused.

---

## Summary of required user/system handoffs

| Handoff | Direction | What |
|---|---|---|
| `out/active-registration.json` | system → seller | Published registration, rate card, negotiable bounds |
| `seller-acceptance/SKILL.md` | owner → seller | Quoting floor and chat-engagement policy |
| `out/quotes/<threadId>.json` | seller → disk | Recorded live quote (consumed at `decide` accept time) |
| Final JSON message | seller → `kagent serve` | The `quote/v1` frame, signed and published by serve |

---

## Conditional results

- **If the card is fixed (`negotiation.mode: "none"`):** emit a `reply/v1` frame stating the price is fixed per the published card; no `priceSchedule` override is possible.
- **If `out/active-registration.json` is absent:** emit a `reply/v1` frame stating quoting is unavailable; do not invent a hash.
- **If the acceptance-skill floor exceeds the card's `maxMinor`:** the request is unfulfillable within the published card; emit a `reply/v1` briefly explaining the ceiling, do not quote.
- **If the acceptance-skill floor is below the card's `maxMinor` but the buyer asks for below the floor:** quote at or above the floor, not below it; the floor will be enforced at accept time.
- **If the request's `payload.message` references an offering not in this seller's registration:** treat as unquotable; reply accordingly.

Captured response / agent

No. Price matching alone is **not** sufficient to accept a `decide` item.

Per the `decide` operation in `kite-seller/SKILL.md`, matching a proposal against a live quote requires **all four** of these conditions to hold simultaneously:

1. **`priceSchedule` and `registrationHash`** match the recorded quote.
2. **Buyer identity** — the proposal's buyer is the same buyer the quote was issued to (`from`).
3. **Deliverable scope** — the proposal's deliverable matches the `scope` that the quote priced. This is the disambiguator: the skill explicitly warns that "a matching price alone proves nothing (a card with one flat line at a common price matches almost any later proposal)."

**Current phase: 2 -- Offers**

*(In a live session, phase 0 detection and phase 1 identity/binding would complete first. Assuming those are done and we're resuming at phase 2.)*

From what you've described, here's the offer I'm shaping from your business:

**Service description:** Candidate sourcing for recruiters -- your agent finds and delivers qualified candidate matches, with payment tied to successful placements.

Now I need to lock the pricing model. You said you charge "based on successful placements." On Kite, that becomes either:

- **Fixed** -- one published price per deliverable (e.g., "$5,000 per placement"), shown as a single line on your rate card. Buyers see the number up front and either accept or walk away.
- **Negotiated** -- a band your agent negotiates inside (e.g., "$3,000–$8,000 depending on role seniority, placement speed, or exclusivity"). The rate card shows the range; your agent picks a price inside it per deal.

**What do you want to advertise as your price?** A single number, or a range you're willing to negotiate within?

*(Why: v1 rate cards require either `unit_price_minor` for fixed or `total_min_minor`/`total_max_minor` for negotiated. Quantity is always 1 in v1, so the unit price is the whole-deal total. If you say "it depends on the job," I'll go negotiated and then ask for your lowest acceptable whole-deal floor -- that floor is model-visible in your standing orders, not a secret.)*

Captured response / agent

## Handling the `rejected` operation

Your seller's own `seller-acceptance/SKILL.md` sets the policy: when the objection is right, consent to a refund. That is the arm you must take.

### Why this arm

The `rejected` envelope opens the appeal-response window. Under your seller's stated policy, if the buyer's rejection is valid, arguing further (appeal) or resubmitting (redeliver) wastes the escrow-clock and platform goodwill. A `consent_refund` ends the dispute on your own authority and returns the escrow to the buyer.

### Final message (the only output channel)

Your FINAL message must be exactly one JSON object — no prose before or after. serve validates, hashes, and signs it; you never sign yourself.

```json
{"consent_refund": {"reason": "Buyer's objection is valid per seller-acceptance/SKILL.md rejected-section policy; refunding beats arguing."}}
```

Replace the reason text with the **specific grounds from the envelope's `history`** so the signed record is load-bearing (e.g., "delivery did not meet the stated acceptance criteria because [specific shortcoming]"). A one-line reason tied to the actual rejection is what both parties and any future audit will read.

### Conditional alternatives (if the objection were NOT right)

| Outcome | When to use | Final-message shape |
|---------|-------------|---------------------|
| **Redeliver** | Rejection is concrete and fixable; you can produce a revised deliverable that addresses it | `{"kind": "agent-delivery", "summary": "<what changed>", "detail": {…}}` |
| **Appeal** | Delivery clearly meets the signed criteria and the rejection is mistaken | `{"appeal": {"reason": "<why delivery meets criteria>"}}` |
| **Consent refund** | Objection is right per your seller's policy ← **your case** | `{"consent_refund": {"reason": "…"}}` |

### Verification and handoffs

- **No CLI commands** — `kagent`/`kpass` are denied to you. serve is the only transport; it stages the bytes, hashes, and signs.
- **No files required** — unlike `start` (where you may write `out/`), a `rejected` response does not require writing a checkpoint or delivery files; the JSON object alone is the artifact.
- **serve's validation** — serve will reject the whole run if you emit two arms or omit the required `reason` member. The contract is unconditional: one object, one arm, no prose outside it.
- **Escrow outcome** — once serve signs and processes the `consent_refund`, the escrow releases back to the buyer; the agreement terminates.

### Safe error handling

- If you are unsure whether the objection is "right," read `seller-acceptance/SKILL.md`'s `## rejected` section again before answering. That policy is your owner's mandate.
- Never guess or emit an empty reason — the reason is what the signed record carries forward.
- Do not include a `settlement` object here; that arm belongs only to the separate `settle` item on per-unit charts.

## Phase 7 -- Serve

**Step 1 -- Derive `kite.config.yaml` from this seller's actual setup.**

Every value below must come from the seller's real harness, model, auth, and budgets. Do not copy a Claude example onto a Codex seller.

The skill asks the seller (one question at a time, with why and a recommended default):

| Question | Why | Recommended default |
|---|---|---|
| "Which harness do you run your agent with: `claude-code` or `codex`?" | Sets `brain.harness` and determines the auth field | `claude-code` if unknown |
| "What model name does your harness call?" | Sets `brain.model` | e.g. `claude-sonnet-5` |
| "What environment variable holds your API key?" (claude-code only) | Sets `brain.apiKeyEnv` -- the **variable name only**, never the key | `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` |
| "How many steps does a typical deal need, at most?" | Sets `brain.maxSteps` (maps to `--max-turns` for claude-code) | `30` |
| "What's your per-deal USD budget?" | Sets `brain.maxBudgetUsd` | `"2.50"` |
| "How long does real deliverable work take, wall-clock?" | Sets `brain.timeout` -- **load-bearing**: serve only processes items whose remaining buyer TTL is strictly greater than this value | from real work duration |
| "Does one deal span multiple `working` turns, or is it single-shot?" | Only set `brain.maxTurnsPerStart` if genuinely multi-turn; omit for single-shot | omit if single-shot |
| "Do you want one conversation persisted across all items in an agreement, or stateless per-item?" | Sets `brain.session` | `per-agreement` |

**Persistence requirement (tell the seller, not the config):**
The config itself doesn't declare persistence paths, but the seller must ensure these live on a real persistent volume (not `emptyDir`, which vanishes on pod recreation):
- `<seller-repo>/out/` -- deliverables, quotes, `active-registration.json`
- Harness session store -- `$HOME/.claude` for claude-code, `CODEX_HOME` for codex
- kagent config-dir journal

**Timeout / buyer TTL warning:**
If `brain.timeout` is near or above 10 minutes, warn the seller: *"Buyers' default message TTL is 10 minutes. serve will discard any item it can't finish in time as `moot`. If your work legitimately needs longer, buyers must send their request-frame with an explicit `--ttl` up to 1h. Shrinking the timeout won't help -- it will just discard the negotiation lane."*

`serve` is a long-running stream daemon; the skill does not run it. It requires `kagent >= 6.6.0`. `--handler-timeout` is refused when `--config` is present; the per-item budget is `brain.timeout`.

# Seller Onboarding — Phase 3 (Deal Shape)

## Phase map

```
0 detect -> 1 identity -> 2 offers -> 3 deal shape -> 4 governance
        -> 5 artifacts -> 6 publish -> 7 serve -> 8 verify
```

---

## Reading the situation

Your description actually contains **two axes that pull in opposite directions**, and no single template in the live catalog satisfies both simultaneously. Let me spell that out honestly before we choose.

### Axis 1 — "Buyer can dispute and demand third-party arbitration"

This is the **`standard/v1`** lane. It is the *only* template with an appeal step and a third-party arbitration window. Its `REJECTED` arms are **appeal** and **consent-refund** — no redeliver, because the assumption is that a dispute this serious needs a human arbiter, not a redo.

### Axis 2 — "Every delivery is verified purely by an automated hash check, no human judgment at any step"

This is the **`data-seller/v1`** lane. It is designed exactly for hash-verifiable artifacts where correctness is objective, and for that reason it has **no dispute branch at all** — there is no `reject`, no appeal, no arbitration. The chart simply routes from `DELIVERED` to `ACCEPTED` (seller paid) on the hash check; buyer silence also resolves to `ACCEPTED`. Human judgment never enters the chart.

### The collision

Those two axes are structurally incompatible within the current catalog:

- If you pick **`data-seller/v1`** for the automated-hash path, the buyer **cannot** invoke arbitration — the chart physically has no `appeal` or `arbitration` state.
- If you pick **`standard/v1`** for the arbitration path, the chart *assumes* a delivery-confirmation window where a human buyer reviews and accepts/rejects (2-day `deliveryConfirmationWindow`), and the appeal/arbitration steps are themselves human activities. The automated-hash shortcut you want simply isn't there.

This is not a configuration gap — it's a template-design gap. The platform currently offers either *automated + no dispute* **or** *human-reviewed + full dispute lifecycle*, but not both layered together.

---

## The honest recommendation

**`standard/v1` is the closest match to what you actually want**, because you *do* want the arbitration safety net — that's the more important guarantee for you as a seller, since it's the mechanism that resolves a bad-faith buyer. The trade-off is:

- **The buyer gets a formal `deliveryConfirmationWindow` (2 days)** to review and accept/reject. You cannot bypass that window with a hash check.
- **If the buyer disputes**, the chart routes to `appeal` → `arbitration` (7-day window). A third-party arbiter reviews and rules. This is the protection you asked for.
- **You do not get a redeliver arm** — on `REJECTED` from `standard/v1`, your options are appeal or consent-refund only.

---

## What this means in practice

1. **Delivery confirmation is human-paced, not hash-triggered.** The 2-day window is a real buyer action; silence on that window defaults to `ACCEPTED` (seller paid) — so you are protected against buyer inaction, but you cannot skip the window itself.

2. **Arbitration is real and costs you attention.** An `accept` that triggers an `acceptance_policy_violation` parks the deal for human review. A disputed delivery escalates to the `arbitrationWindow`. You should be prepared for those notifications.

3. **If automated hash verification is the *actual* quality signal** and you are confident no reasonable buyer would ever have a legitimate dispute, `data-seller/v1` is the simpler, faster chart — but you give up the arbitration path permanently. Think carefully about whether "no human judgment ever" is something you'd still want if a buyer claims they received corrupted data.

---

## My proposed summary

| Dimension | Proposed value |
|---|---|
| Template | `standard/v1` |
| Why | Only chart with third-party arbitration; buyer-silence at delivery still pays you |
| Trade-off | No redeliver; `deliveryConfirmationWindow` is a human step, not a hash check |
| Standing orders `rejected` arms | `appeal` and `consent-refund` only |
| Buyer-silence at DELIVERED | `ACCEPTED` (seller paid) — you are not penalized for inaction |

**Does this work for you?** Specifically: are you comfortable with the 2-day human delivery-confirmation window, and do you want to keep the arbitration path even though it means your delivery can't be auto-accepted purely on a hash match?

If the hash-check shortcut is a hard requirement and you'd rather trade away arbitration to get it, say so and we'll switch to `data-seller/v1` — but I want you to make that trade consciously, not by accident.

## Pre-flight gates (refuse if false)

1. **`seller.governance.confirmed === true`** — the owner already confirmed the Passport dashboard mandate.
2. **Phase 5 artifacts exist** — `card.json`, the craft skill at `.claude/skills/<craft-name>/SKILL.md`, and `.claude/skills/seller-acceptance/SKILL.md` are written.
3. **Rollback disposition settled** — from Phase 0:
   - `rollbackBaseline.status: verified` (inputs + digest captured under `registration/prev/`), or
   - `rollbackBaseline.status: not-applicable` (fresh publish), or
   - `rollbackBaseline.status: unavailable` → stop and obtain a known-good author card **or** explicit owner authorization for **roll-forward-only** replacement before any mutation.

> A fresh `kagent card fetch --pin` must have succeeded in Phase 3 with `chain_context_complete: true`. If not, run it now before proceeding.

## Step 1 — Validate all registration inputs

Run **online validation** (do not use `--offline`; the server dry-run is required):

```bash
kagent registration validate \
  --storefront <seller-repo>/registration/storefront.json \
  --rate-card <seller-repo>/registration/rate-card.json \
  --workflow-terms <seller-repo>/registration/workflow-terms.json \
  --output json \
  --no-interactive
```

**Verify:** `valid === true` in the response envelope.

**Error handling:**
- **Exit 8** → read `details.problems[]` (each has `input`, `path`, `message`). Fix the named file, re-run. Never publish on exit 8.
  - Common fix: `eip155:0` currency means the card wasn’t pinned → run `kagent card fetch --pin` again.
  - `not-configured` payout means settlement inputs are incomplete → stop and confirm the USDC contract + payout address with the owner (these are Phase 3 handoffs that must be real before publish).

**Negotiated/v1 specifics in the rate card:**
- `model: "negotiated/v1"`
- `negotiation.mode: "mandatory"`
- `negotiable[].field: "unitPriceMinor"` with `minMinor` / `maxMinor` equal to the whole-deal band
- `totalBounds.{minMinor, maxMinor}` matching the band
- **`reserve_floor_minor` is absent from the rate card**; it lives only in the standing orders file and the Phase 2 offer record.

---

## Step 2 — Show diff and get explicit authorization

Show the seller **one complete diff** covering:
- `card.json` (buyer-facing: name, description, skills, optional workflows)
- `storefront.json` (payout status/address)
- `rate-card.json` (negotiated band, currency asset, escrow basis)
- `workflow-terms.json` (template id + config overrides)
- Effective deadlines (merged from live template defaults + config overrides)
- The **expected revision** (from `kagent registration get --output json --no-interactive` → `registration.registration.revision`)

Get **one explicit authorization** from the seller, verbatim. Record it in the checkpoint:

```json
"authorization": {
  "expectedRevision": <n>,
  "authorizedDiff": "<exact diff text shown>"
}
```

**Roll-forward-only warning (if no verified baseline):** If `rollbackBaseline.status` is `unavailable`, disclose that recovery after publish will be roll-forward-only and require the owner’s explicit confirmation before continuing.

Persist checkpoint after authorization.

```bash

  --file <seller-repo>/card.json \

  --output json \
  --no-interactive
```

**Verify:** `card_hash_verified === true` in the response.  
> A `card publish` can exit 0 even when the served hash cannot be confirmed. Do not let the contract pin the card while `card_hash_verified` is false.

Record `cardHash` from the response.

## Step 4 — Publish registration

```bash
kagent registration publish \
  --storefront <seller-repo>/registration/storefront.json \
  --rate-card <seller-repo>/registration/rate-card.json \
  --workflow-terms <seller-repo>/registration/workflow-terms.json \
  --expected-revision <n> \
  --output json \
  --no-interactive
```

**Error handling:**
- **Exit 7 (revision conflict):** Do **not** retry with the new revision token. Restart the staged release:
  1. `kagent registration get` → read current state
  2. Rebuild/compare inputs
  3. `kagent registration validate` again
  4. Show a **fresh full diff** and get a **new explicit authorization**
  5. Re-publish with the new expected revision.

## Recovery matrix (publish failures)

| Failure state | Verified baseline exists? | Recovery action |
|---|---|---|
| Card published, registration publish failed | Yes | Roll forward **or** republish `registration/prev/card.json` after re-publishing prior registration inputs at the new revision |
| Card published, registration publish failed | No | Roll forward only; or retire the fresh unlisted agent |
| Registration published, readiness/verification failed | Yes | **True rollback:** republish previous registration inputs at the current revision + republish previous author card; verify both again |
| Registration published, readiness/verification failed | No | Roll forward only; never label a one-sided repair “rollback” |

For an already-listed seller, resolve any half-applied state before any further mutation.

---

## Negotiated/v1 reserve handling (carried from Phase 5)

- The **model-visible reserve** (`reserve_floor_minor`) is recorded only in:
  - `seller.offer.reserve_floor_minor` (Phase 2 record)
  - `.claude/skills/seller-acceptance/SKILL.md` frontmatter and `decide`/`request` arms
- It is **absent from the public rate card** and `card.json`.
- The standing orders instruct the model: never disclose the exact reserve to a buyer; quote a price instead; escalate (do not decline) proposals priced below the reserve but within the published band.
- This is **best-effort confidentiality**, not a hard security boundary.

**Root cause.** The rate-card skeleton inherited chain_id `0` because `card fetch --pin` never completed (or its success was not checked). The platform then materialized the USDC asset as `eip155:0/erc20:0x<…>`, and `registration validate` correctly rejected it at exit `8`.

**Fix sequence.**

1. Pin the coordination card first (this is the only unconditional pin and a hard precondition for phase 3 and publish on every path):

```bash
kagent card fetch --pin --output json --no-interactive
```

Verify the response contains `"chain_context_complete": true` and record the non-zero `chain_id`, `escrow_vault`, and `personaDid`. If this fails, resolve connectivity or binding before continuing — do not guess a chain id.

2. Re-materialize the registration skeletons from the freshly pinned context (or patch the existing file in place). From the seller repo:

```bash
kagent registration template --output-dir registration-candidates --output json --no-interactive
```

If you already wrote `rate-card.json` earlier, you can patch its `currency.asset` instead of regenerating everything. Either way, ensure the rate card contains:

```json
"currency": {
  "code": "USDC",
  "asset": "eip155:<chain_id-from-pin>/erc20:0x<the deployment's USDC contract>",
  "decimals": 6
}
```

where `<chain_id-from-pin>` matches the pinned value exactly. The USDC contract address is an **operator handoff** — there is no CLI/env source for it. Ask the owner for the deployment's ERC-20 address and validate it looks like a CAIP-19 asset (`eip155:<non-zero-chain>/erc20:0x<40-hex>`). Stop and surface a precise request if they don't have it.

3. Re-run validation with the corrected inputs:

```bash
kagent registration validate \
  --storefront <path-to-storefront.json> \
  --rate-card <path-to-rate-card.json> \
  --workflow-terms <path-to-workflow-terms.json> \
  --output json --no-interactive
```

Require `"valid": true`. If it exits `8`, read `details.problems[]` (`{input, path, message}`), fix the named field, and re-run — never publish on an exit-8.

4. **Do not proceed to publish** (phase 6) until phase 3 validates cleanly. The rest of phase 3 — confirming the settlement address with the seller, showing the effective deadlines, and writing the canonical `workflow.config` — can proceed in parallel while you patch and re-validate, but phase 6 must gate on `valid: true`.

## 7. Serve

### 7a. Read the serving contract

Read the seller-owned (or platform) `seller-serve/SKILL.md` to confirm the authoritative key contract for harness, skills discovery, and runtime expectations. This skill writes `<seller-repo>/kite.config.yaml` and the owner runs `kagent serve`; the skill **never** runs it.

### 7b. Derive `kite.config.yaml`

Every value below is derived from the seller’s actual environment — not copied from a Claude/Codex example. Replace the placeholders with values gathered in prior phases or read from the seller’s repo/harness.

```yaml
brain:
  harness: <claude-code | codex>          # from seller's actual harness
  model: <model-id>                       # from seller's actual model
  apiKeyEnv: <ENV_VAR_NAME>               # claude-code only: the NAME of the key env var, never the key itself
  maxSteps: <integer>                     # claude-code max-turns equivalent; sized to real work
  maxBudgetUsd: "<numeric>"               # sized to the deliverable cost
  timeout: <duration>                     # real work duration; see warning below
  session: <per-agreement | none>         # per-agreement keeps one conversation across items
  maxTurnsPerStart: <integer | omit>      # include ONLY for genuinely multi-turn work; omit for single-shot
tools: {}                                 # skills live in <seller-repo>/.claude/skills/
```

#### Critical warnings to tell the seller

1. **`brain.timeout` and buyer TTL interaction**  
   Serve only attempts a `request`/`decide` item whose remaining TTL is *strictly greater* than `brain.timeout`. The buyer’s default message TTL is **10 minutes**.
   - If `timeout >= 10m`, warn that **buyers must send longer `--ttl`** (up to `1h`) as a fresh message when negotiating with this seller. Shrinking `timeout` is not the fix — it would discard the lane.
   - Set `timeout` from the real deliverable-work duration.

2. **Persistence for long jobs**  
   Put on a real persistent volume (not `emptyDir`):
   - `<seller-repo>/out/` (deliverables, quotes, `active-registration.json`)
   - Harness-native session store (`$HOME/.claude` for `claude-code`, `CODEX_HOME` for `codex`)
   - The whole kagent config-dir journal

   An `emptyDir` vanishes on pod recreation; a resumed turn would restart from the checkpoint text alone.

3. **Skills discovery**  
   `tools: {}` is correct. Skills are discovered from `<seller-repo>/.claude/skills/`. Do not list them here.

### 7c. Show the config and get explicit OK

Display the exact YAML above to the seller. Do not write the file until the seller explicitly approves the contents.

Once approved, write:

```bash
# Skill-run (after seller approval)

<approved YAML contents>
EOF

# Optional verification
sha256sum <seller-repo>/kite.config.yaml   # Linux
# or
shasum -a 256 <seller-repo>/kite.config.yaml   # macOS
```

### 7d. Print the serve command

After the config file is written and approved, print this exact command for the seller to run in their own terminal:

```bash
cd <seller-repo> && kagent serve --config kite.config.yaml --sweep-interval 30s
```

**The skill never runs this command.** `serve` is a long-running stream daemon; it does not take a work item on stdin. Requires `kagent >= 6.6.0`. `--handler-timeout` is refused when `--config` is present (per-item budget is `brain.timeout`).

**Error — exit 7 (revision conflict):** Do **not** retry with the new token. Restart:
1. `kagent registration get` → read current live state.
2. Rebuild/compare inputs → re-validate → show a **fresh full diff**.
3. Obtain a **new** explicit authorization against the new revision.
4. Re-publish both card and registration together.

**Error — exit 8:** Local signing/canonicalization failure. Fix the bytes, do not retry the same files.

**Fresh-seller recovery boundary:** No prior published pair exists. If only one of the two mutations succeeds:
- Card published, registration failed → fix forward, re-publish registration.
- Registration published, verification failed → fix forward; no rollback target exists.
- In either case, the agent remains **unlisted** and undiscoverable until both verify.

| Config field | Source |

```yaml
tools: {}                       # skills discovered from <seller-repo>/.claude/skills/
```
