**Phase 6 -- Publish: `card_hash_verified: false` handling**

`kagent card publish` exiting `0` while `card_hash_verified` is `false` means the command published bytes but the post-publish re-fetch produced a digest that did not match what was just written. The skill treats this as a hard stop: **do not let a contract pin the card while it stands.** Do not run `registration publish` or any later step. The staged release halts here.

---

### 1. Diagnose before mutating anything else

Read the full `card publish` response envelope first (it should carry `card_hash`, `published_card_hash` or equivalent, and `card_hash_verified`). Then inspect what the platform is actually serving:

```bash
kagent directory card <did> --source platform --output json --no-interactive
```

Recompute the local digest and compare it against both the publish response and the platform-served card:

```bash
shasum -a 256 card.json    # macOS
# or
sha256sum card.json        # Linux
```

---

### 2. Decision tree

| Finding | Action |
|---|---|
| **Local `card.json` is well-formed and intentional, but platform serves different bytes** | Likely a platform/cache or encoding glitch. Retry `card publish` once after a brief wait. If the retry also returns `card_hash_verified: false`, stop and surface the platform inconsistency to the owner; do not keep retrying blindly. |
| **Local `card.json` differs from what the seller approved** | Fix `card.json`, re-run `kagent registration validate`, show a fresh diff, obtain new explicit authorization, then re-publish. |
| **Transient error** (response fields show partial write, network blip, etc.) | Retry `card publish` after confirming local `card.json` is unchanged. |

---

### 3. Recovery posture (depends on rollback baseline)

**Fresh publish** (`rollbackBaseline.status: not-applicable`):
- There is no prior published pair to restore. Keep the agent **unlisted** (it is still unlisted because listing only happens in step 7).
- Fix forward (repair `card.json`, re-validate, re-authorize, re-publish), or retire the half-published unlisted agent. Do not claim rollback exists.

**Reconfigure with verified baseline** (`rollbackBaseline.status: verified`, `registration/prev/card.json` present with matching digest):
- Republish the previous author card to restore a known-good state:
  ```bash
  kagent card publish --file registration/prev/card.json --output json --no-interactive
  ```
- Then investigate the new `card.json` issue separately. Only after a verified card is live should you consider republishing the registration.

**Reconfigure without verified baseline** (`rollbackBaseline.status: unavailable`):
- No trusted prior card exists. The only safe paths are **roll-forward-only** (fix the current `card.json` and re-publish) or retire. Never manufacture a baseline after the fact.

---

### 4. Checkpoint and handoffs

- Update `<seller-repo>/.kite-onboarding.json`: record the `card_hash_verified: false` outcome, the observed hashes, and the chosen disposition (`fix-forward`, `rollback-to-prev`, or `retire`). Do **not** advance `completedPhases` to include `6` or `7`.
- Tell the seller plainly: the publish command ran, but the platform bytes could not be confirmed, so nothing after card publish is safe to run. The agent remains **unlisted** and unusable until this is resolved.
- If the owner must intervene (dashboard retirement, known-good card supply, or platform support), hand off explicitly.

---

### 5. What never happens here

- No `registration publish` is run.
- No claim that the card is live or verified.
- No progression to serve/verify (phase 7/8).
- No silent retry that could pin a registration to an unverified card hash.

Once a verified `card publish` (`card_hash_verified: true`) is achieved and checked, the release resumes at step 4 (`registration publish`) with a fresh expected-revision read and explicit re-authorization.

## Phase 3 -- Deal shape: recruiting/v1

### Template match

Your description maps cleanly to **`recruiting/v1`** -- *"Candidate-sourcing delivery with buyer confirmation and no dispute branch."* No do-overs, no appeal, no arbitration: the chart has only `ACCEPTED` → `DELIVERED` → done, plus a `consent_refund` arm. That matches your "delivered-is-delivered" framing.

### Critical warning -- read this before confirming

`recruiting/v1` has a behavior that is the **opposite** of what "delivered is delivered" suggests:

| Template | What happens when the buyer goes quiet after delivery |
|---|---|
| `data-seller/v1` | **Seller is paid** -- silence resolves `ACCEPTED` |
| `recruiting/v1` | **Buyer is refunded, seller is NOT paid** -- silence defaults |

On `recruiting/v1`, payment requires the **buyer to actively confirm** delivery. If the buyer never confirms and never complains, the deal defaults and you are unpaid. This is not a bug; it is the chart's design for recruiting where confirmation signals a successful placement.

**Does this trade-off work for your business?** If you expected "delivered means paid regardless," `standard/v1` may be a better fit (silence pays the seller there). If you are comfortable driving buyer confirmation and the silence-default is acceptable, we proceed with `recruiting/v1`.

### What you are committing to

- **No rejection lane.** The buyer cannot reject and ask for a redo; there is no redeliver, no appeal, no arbitration. Once you deliver, the only buyer move is `consent_refund` (mutual).
- **Confirmation is on the buyer.** Your craft skill should nudge the buyer to confirm when you deliver; otherwise the timer runs and you are not paid.
- **Windows** (from the live chart):
  - **Funding window:** 30 minutes -- buyer must fund within 30m of acceptance
  - **Delivery window:** 1 hour -- you must deliver within 1h of funding
  - **Delivery confirmation window:** 1 hour -- buyer has 1h after delivery to confirm (or it defaults)

### Pricing model

We need your pricing to finish the rate card and standing orders:

> **"What do you want to advertise as your price?"**

One number = **fixed** (one whole-deal price, no negotiation).  
A band you would negotiate within = **negotiated** (you tell me the min and max whole-deal price, and optionally a reserve floor you would actually accept).

This is the whole-deliverable price, not a per-candidate rate (per-unit is GOK-1302, deferred).

### Workflow config (no overrides needed)

Because `recruiting/v1` has no rejection lane, **`maxRedeliveries` is meaningless here** -- I will not configure it. The canonical config is minimal: only window overrides if you want to shift them away from the chart defaults. The defaults are:

- `fundingWindow`: 1800s (30m)
- `deliveryWindow`: 3600s (1h)
- `deliveryConfirmationWindow`: 3600s (1h)

Do you want to keep these as-is, or shift any of them?

# Phase 5 — Standing Orders: `standard/v1` then `coding/v1`

From `standing-orders-template.md` + `template-characteristics.md`. The `REJECTED` arms are the only difference between these two rows; everything else (decide, request, floor logic) is structural.

---

## Template-characters check (read live, not guessed)

| Template | `REJECTED` arms |
|---|---|
| `standard/v1` | **Appeal / consent-refund** — **no redeliver** |
| `coding/v1` | **Redeliver / consent-refund** (unique extra: `amend`) |

`coding/v1` is the *only* row that supports `amend` in-flight, but that verb is owned by `kagent`/`seller-fulfill`, not by the standing orders — the standing orders only cover the seller's business-decision response to a rejection. `amend` is therefore noted as a capability caveat, not emitted as a standing-order arm.

---

## `standard/v1` — `seller-acceptance/SKILL.md`

```markdown
---
name: seller-acceptance
description: Your agent's standing orders — your business judgment for each buyer interaction. You own this file; it lives in your repo.
---

# Standing Orders

## decide

Accept a proposal when all of these hold:
- It matches your service: <seller.offer.service_description>.
- Its price clears your floor (<the shared floor for this offer type> minor units).
- It fits the deal shape you offer (standard/v1).

<seller.governance.escalation_rule, verbatim — escalate these to the owner>

Decline anything else (out of scope, or work you never take).

## request

For pre-deal chat, quote asks, clarifications, and sample requests:
- Quote per your published card, and never quote below your floor (<the shared floor> minor units). A fixed card has one price; a negotiated card is quoted within its published band. Never disclose the exact reserve/floor in a reply; quote a price instead.
- Answer briefly and on-topic. Don't spend the agent's tokens on open-ended free chat with no payment in sight.

## rejected

If a buyer rejects your delivery:
- **Appeal** — only when your delivery clearly meets what was signed for. Do not appeal a rejection that names a real, fixable gap in the deliverable.
- **Consent to a refund** if the objection is right, or if finishing would cost far more than the deal is worth — an early honest refund protects your reputation more than a bad delivery.
```

**Notes for the seller:**
- `standard/v1` has no `revise` arm — the chart cannot redeliver from `REJECTED`. Once a buyer rejects, the only seller moves are appeal or consent-refund.
- `standard/v1` *does* support formal arbitration (`arbitrationWindow: 604800` / 7 days). If a dispute escalates past the appeal stage, that path is automated by the platform; the standing orders cover only the seller's own decisions.
- The floor value (`<the shared floor>`) is filled from phase 2: fixed → `unit_price_minor`; negotiated → `total_min_minor` (or `reserve_floor_minor` if the negotiated offer carries one).

name: seller-acceptance

# Standing Orders

## decide

- It matches your service: <seller.offer.service_description>.
- Its price clears your floor (<the shared floor for this offer type> minor units).
- It fits the deal shape you offer (coding/v1).

<seller.governance.escalation_rule, verbatim — escalate these to the owner>

Decline anything else (out of scope, or work you never take).

## request

For pre-deal chat, quote asks, clarifications, and sample requests:
- Quote per your published card, and never quote below your floor (<the shared floor> minor units). A fixed card has one price; a negotiated card is quoted within its published band. Never disclose the exact reserve/floor in a reply; quote a price instead.
- Answer briefly and on-topic. Don't spend the agent's tokens on open-ended free chat with no payment in sight.

## rejected

If a buyer rejects your delivery:
- **Revise once** — only if the rejection names something concrete and fixable, and you still have redeliveries remaining in your `maxRedeliveries` budget. If either condition is false, skip to consent-refund below. (This arm exists because coding/v1 supports bounded redeliveries from REJECTED; it does not exist on standard/v1.)
- **Consent to a refund** if the objection is right, or if finishing would cost far more than the deal is worth — an early honest refund protects your reputation more than a bad delivery.
```

**Notes for the seller:**
- `coding/v1` is the only template that supports `amend` (in-flight term changes). That verb is driven by buyer request through the platform; the standing orders don't invoke it — the model should surface an amendment request to the owner rather than act on it directly.
- `maxRedeliveries` is materialized by the platform at publish time (default `2` for coding/v1); the serving model reads the resolved value from the workflow and must honour it. The "revise once" arm above says *at most once per rejection* regardless — a second rejection on the same deal has no further redeliver path and falls through to consent-refund.
- Windows to note: `deliveryWindow 604800` (7 days), `deliveryConfirmationWindow 604800` (7 days). Both are long — the buyer has a week to confirm delivery.

|---|---|

| `kpass identifier claim` | Stop sign because `--type`/handle are immutable; if already claimed, use the existing one. |
| Binding lands `pending` | Terminal wait for owner dashboard approval; do not re-bind or recreate. |
| Binding is `revoked` | Stop; re-init is destructive. |

# Seller Onboarding -- Phase 1: Identity and Binding

Current phase map:

```
0 detect -> 1 identity -> 2 offers -> 3 deal shape -> 4 governance
        -> 5 artifacts -> 6 publish -> 7 serve -> 8 verify

## Phase 0 -- Detect and Resume

Before doing anything else, run these two commands to determine if there is existing state to resume from.

**Command 1 -- local runtime state:**

```bash
kagent status --output json --no-interactive
```

**Branch on the result:**

| `key.present` | `binding.bound` | `binding.status` | `backend.reachable` | Action |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `false` | — | — | any | No key → proceed to Phase 1 create/bind |
| `true` | `true` | `active` | `true` | Active binding → skip identity, read registration, resume at first incomplete phase |
| `true` | `true` | `pending` | `true` | Pending binding → **terminal wait**: tell the owner the binding is pending dashboard approval; do NOT re-ask UID/name, do NOT re-create or re-bind; resume once `active` |
| `true` | `true` | `revoked` | any | **Stop** -- re-init is destructive |
| `true` | `false` | `unbound` | any | Key present, no identity → proceed to Phase 1 |
| any | any | any | `false` | **Stop** -- surface connectivity; do not treat as "nothing set up" |

## Owner-Only Inventory Read (when identity work is needed)

Only when the branch above requires identity work (no-key, unbound), read the owner's existing seller records:

```bash
kpass user agents --agent-type seller --output json --no-interactive
```

- If the result contains a suitable existing record (match by exact `id`, `did`, or `uid`), reuse it -- **do not create a new identity**.
- If no suitable record exists, a new identity must be created → proceed to the onboarding bootstrap below.

> **Owner-login note:** if this command exits 3 (auth error), the owner session has expired. `kpass login` alone does nothing (it prints help and exits 0). **Current phase: 2 -- Offers**

You've told me your agent's pricing is negotiated — meaning buyers propose a price inside a band, rather than paying one fixed headline number. That fits `negotiated/v1`, the single-deliverable negotiated shape.

Before we set your reserve, you asked the right question:

**Your reserve is not a secret, and no platform mechanism in v1 makes it one.**

Here is exactly why:

- Your serving model (the model that answers buyer messages) reads `seller-acceptance/SKILL.md` at runtime. That file contains the reserve floor so the model can enforce it and route below-reserve proposals to you instead of declining them automatically.
- The buyer's messages are untrusted input into that same model. The model is instructed *not* to disclose the reserve, but a prompt-driven model cannot offer a hard confidentiality boundary — a determined buyer can probe for it.
- The platform does not enforce a deterministic secret floor in v1; that tracking is deferred to a future improvement (GOK-1302 covers per-unit rate cards and related mechanics).

What you *do* get: the reserve is absent from the public rate card, and the model is told never to quote or confirm the exact number. Treat that as best-effort business policy, not a security guarantee.

---

**Given that, do you still want a reserve?**

- **Yes — keep a reserve.** You accept that it is model-visible and tell the model never to disclose it. I'll record `reserve_floor_minor` and wire it into standing orders as the model-visible floor. *(Recommended if you want below-band proposals auto-parked for your approval rather than auto-declined.)*
- **No — negotiate the full band only.** Drop the reserve. The published `totalBounds` become the floor; anything below the band bottom is declined automatically by the mandate. Simpler, and the number stays off your own skill file.

# Post-Listing Verification with Unverified Owner Inventory

## Phase Map (current: phase 8 -- Verify)

```
0 detect -> 1 identity -> 2 offers -> 3 deal shape -> 4 governance
        -> 5 artifacts -> 6 publish -> 7 serve -> 8 verify  ← YOU ARE HERE
```

---

## What "unverified owner inventory row fields" means here

`kpass user agents --agent-type seller` returns server-owned `agents[]` rows with `id`, `did`, `uid`, `name`, etc. Those fields are owned by Passport's control plane, not by this skill, and the reference explicitly states:

> *"Owner inventory may corroborate it, but the flow does not depend on an unverified row field."*

For a **post-listing** seller, the owner-inventory read is **optional** here -- it served its purpose back in phase 1 (deciding whether to reuse or create an identity). Phase 8 verification has its own authoritative proofs that do not depend on those row fields.

**Do not gate verification on `kpass user agents` or treat its row fields as a listing proof.** Its only legitimate use at this stage is optional corroboration if the owner happens to run it.

---

## Step-by-step verification (phase 8)

**Note:** Phase 8 is currently externally blocked by **GOK-1272** (buyer Activation signing). The steps below describe the full verification handoff the seller completes once that platform bug is resolved. Settlement cannot complete until then.

### 1. Reconstruct current state (no owner-login gate)

```bash
# Always first; phase 8 resume must not block on owner auth
kagent status --output json --no-interactive
```

Branch on the binding:

| `binding.status` | Action |
|---|---|
| `active` | Continue to step 2 |
| `pending` | **Terminal handoff** -- dashboard approval still outstanding; do not proceed to verify |
| `revoked` | **Stop** -- re-init is destructive; retire the agent instead |
| `unbound` | Return to phase 1 |
| `backend.reachable: false` | Stop; surface connectivity |

An active seller does **not** require an owner login to reach verify. Skip `kpass status`/`kpass me` and `kpass user agents` unless identity work is needed.

### 2. Read live registration

```bash
kagent registration get --output json --no-interactive
```

Confirm:

- `registration.registration.status` -- should be the live published state
- `registration.projection.readiness.ok === true` -- if `false`, read `readiness.reasons[]` and fix each named issue before continuing; do not skip to the Playground
- `registration.projection.verification` -- confirm it is `verified` or the expected live state
- `registration.projection.offerings[]` -- note `workflowHash` for step 5

### 3. Re-confirm governance mandate with the owner

The dashboard mandate has **no readback** -- a consistency drift (independent dashboard edit) is undetectable from here. Even with a `confirmed: true` checkpoint flag, the skill must re-confirm mandate values before verification:

Ask the owner:

> "Please confirm in your Passport dashboard that your acceptance policy currently reads:
> - Template allowlist: `[<seller.offer.template_id>]`
> - Floor: <negotiated reserve or total_min_minor, or "none" for fixed>
> - Ceiling: <total_max_minor, or the fixed price>
> - Escalation rule: <verbatim standing-orders escalation text>
>
> If any value differs from what we set during onboarding, tell me before we proceed."

Set `governance.confirmed = true` in state **only** after the owner confirms the live values still match. A mismatch forces phase 4 to re-run before any further action.

### 4. Verify the served card

```bash
kagent directory card <agent-did> --source platform --output json --no-interactive
```

Require `card_hash_verified === true`. If this is `false`, the card bytes and the hash pinned during `card fetch --pin` have diverged -- investigate before continuing. Re-pin if needed:

```bash
kagent card fetch --pin --output json --no-interactive
```

Then re-check `chain_context_complete: true` and re-verify the card hash.

### 5. Verify the resolved workflow is live

```bash
kagent directory workflow <agent-did> <workflow-hash> --output json --no-interactive
```

(Use the `workflowHash` from step 2's `registration.projection.offerings[].workflowHash`.)

Confirm the immutable resolved workflow fields:

- `workflow.ownerAgentDid` -- matches the publishing agent
- `workflow.template.{id,hash,chartHash,configurationSchemaHash}` -- matches the template chosen in phase 3
- `workflow.config.{limits,parameters,skippedStates,windows}` -- matches the `workflow.config` the seller approved in phase 3 (effective deadlines, `maxRedeliveries`)
- `workflow.createdAt` -- confirms it was produced from this registration

No `seller` field should appear in the resolved workflow -- its absence is correct.

### 6. Proof that listing actually happened

After publication the owner listed the agent via the dashboard (step 7 of phase 6). Any `kagent directory get <did>` or `<uid>` lookup resolves both listed **and** unlisted agents, so it proves nothing about visibility. The only listed-only surface is:

```bash
kagent directory search --kind seller [--query "<display-name-fragment>"] --limit 20 --output json --no-interactive
```

Follow `has_more` / `next_command` if the seller does not appear on the first page. Require an **exact** `did`/`uid` match in `agents[]`.

**Owner-inventory corroboration (optional, not authoritative):** The owner may optionally run `kpass user agents --agent-type seller` and check that the returned row's `did` or `uid` matches. This corroborates the record but cannot substitute for the `directory search` exact match -- if the row fields are unverified, this corroboration adds no proof. Do not mark listing complete on owner confirmation alone.

### 7. Rewrite `out/active-registration.json` post-listing

The served `cardHash` changes when the agent transitions from unlisted to listed (the platform-held hash covers `visibility`). After the listing proof:

```bash
# Re-read the live registration to capture the updated cardHash
kagent registration get --output json --no-interactive
```

Persist the full response to:

```
<seller-repo>/out/active-registration.json
```

The serving model (`seller-serve`/`kite-seller`) reads this file and the `registrationHash` to quote; it cannot re-read its own registration with the runtime key, so this file must be current.

### 8. Hand off to the Passport web Playground (GOK-1272 blocked)

> **Settlement is blocked by GOK-1272** (buyer Activation signing). The skill prepares and hands off verification; it does not claim a proven end-to-end deal until that platform bug is fixed.

Give the seller these instructions:

1. Open the **Passport web Playground** (not a CLI improvised buyer agent).
2. Run **one real deal** against their own listed agent: send a proposal, accept it, fund the escrow, deliver, confirm.
3. Observe the full lifecycle through `DELIVERED` (and `ACCEPTED` / `DEFAULTED` depending on the template).
4. Report back the outcome, screenshots, and any error codes.
5. Settlement will remain incomplete until GOK-1272 is resolved -- do not mark the verification as proven until then.

**Never improvise a buyer agent in this conversation.** The Playground is the only authorized verification path.

---

## Summary: what changes when owner inventory row fields are unverified

| Step | Normally uses owner inventory? | When row fields are unverified |
|---|---|---|
| Phase 1 (identity creation) | Yes -- choose reuse vs create | Return to phase 1 if identity work is needed; confirm exact `id`/`did`/`uid` against dashboard |
| Phase 0 detect | Optional inventory read | Still optional; don't gate active-runtime resume on it |
| Phase 8 listing proof | `directory search` is authoritative; inventory corroborates | Use `directory search` exact `did`/`uid` match as sole proof; skip inventory corroboration if fields are unreliable |
| Governance re-confirm | Owner confirms against dashboard | Always re-confirm; dashboard is the only source of truth for mandate values |
| Settlement inputs | Owner supplies USDC contract + payout address | No change; never invent these values |

The verification flow is fully executable without owner inventory row field verification. The owner-inventory read is an optional corroboration layer; its row fields are never the authoritative proof of listing or readiness.

On Linux, use `sha256sum` (the first whitespace-delimited field is the 64-hex digest), then persist only that digest into the checkpoint — never the filename-bearing full output.

## How this works

`references/commands.md` already covers both families explicitly:

> `sha256` on local files is the first whitespace-delimited field from `shasum -a 256 <file>` (macOS) or `sha256sum <file>` (Linux)

Both `shasum` and `sha256sum` are listed in `allowed-tools`, so this is a supported, non-invented path.

---

## Exact Linux commands

**Compute and capture the digest for any checkpoint file:**

```bash
sha256sum <seller-repo>/registration/prev/card.json
```

Example output:
```
a3f2b8c91d4e5067...  /path/to/seller-repo/registration/prev/card.json
```

The 64-hex digest is `a3f2b8c91d4e5067...` (first whitespace-delimited field).

**Store only the digest** into the checkpoint JSON field:

```json
{
  "inputs": {
    "storefront": { "path": "registration/prev/storefront.json", "sha256": "a3f2b8c91d4e5067..." },
    "rateCard":   { "path": "registration/prev/rate-card.json",   "sha256": "b7c4d2..." },
    "workflowTerms": { "path": "registration/prev/workflow-terms.json", "sha256": "f1e9a3..." }
  },
  "artifacts": {
    "cardJson": { "path": "registration/prev/card.json", "sha256": "a3f2b8c91d4e5067..." },
    "cardHash": "<from kagent registration get>"
  }
}
```

**Recompute on resume (re-validate before trusting the checkpoint):**

```bash
sha256sum registration/prev/card.json | awk '{print $1}'
```

Compare the result to the `sha256` value stored in `.kite-onboarding.json` before resuming any phase whose inputs depend on that file.

---

## No `shasum` available — do not install it

`shasum` is a Perl-core utility bundled with macOS; it is not guaranteed on minimal Linux containers. Because both command families are in `allowed-tools`, falling back to `sha256sum` is the **intended Linux path** — no extra packages, no invented helpers, no invented digests.

---

## Key points to carry into the checkpoint

- **Persist only the 64-hex digest**, never the filename. The filename varies across runs and machines.
- **Recompute every digest on resume** — never trust the stored value blindly. Compare recomputed values against the checkpoint before accepting any phase as complete.
- **Platform-side hashes** (`registrationHash`, `cardHash`, `workflowHash`) come from `kagent registration get` / `kagent directory workflow` — they are not computed locally and are stored verbatim.
- **`card.json` author-card digest** must be computed *before* phase 5 overwrites it, so phase 6's rollback disposition has a verified baseline to compare against.
- **`onboarding.json` is sensitive** (it carries mandate values and reserve floors) — gitignore it and do not store it in version control.

# Seller Onboarding — Reconfiguration

**Phase map:** `0 detect -> 1 identity -> 2 offers -> 3 deal shape -> 4 governance -> 5 artifacts -> 6 publish -> 7 serve -> 8 verify`

Current position: **Phase 0 → 3** (active binding detected; phase 3 candidate generation is the next durable mutation).

---

## Phase 0 — Detect, verify, and settle reconfigure rollback posture

### Step 0.1 — Local runtime state

Run the detection gate first:

```bash
kagent status --output json --no-interactive
```

Branch on the response:
- `backend.reachable: false` → stop, surface connectivity.
- `key.present: true, binding.bound: true, binding.status: "active"` → **expected path**; proceed.
- `binding.status: "pending"` → terminal handoff; wait for owner dashboard approval.
- `binding.status: "revoked"` → stop; re-init is destructive.
- Any other combination → escalate before mutating.

### Step 0.2 — Live registration read

An active binding must have its registration read before anything else:

```bash
kagent registration get --output json --no-interactive
```

Record from the envelope:
- `registration.registration.{revision, registrationHash, agentDid, status}`
- `registration.projection.{cardHash, verification, readiness.{ok, reasons}, offerings[].{workflowHash, workflowTemplateHash, workflowConfigHash}}`

Exit `4` = no active registration yet (treat as "nothing published," not an error).

### Step 0.3 — Checkpoint read and integrity check

Read `<seller-repo>/.kite-onboarding.json`. Validate:
1. `checkpointVersion` is present and parsable.
2. `target.did` matches the live binding's DID exactly.
3. `target.configDir` matches the local `kagent` config directory.
4. The checkpointed `agent.uid`, `offer.*`, `workflow.*`, and `governance.*` values are the ones you intend to reconfigure around.

If any of these fail → stop and surface the mismatch; do not silently re-derive from a corrupted checkpoint.

### Step 0.4 — Reconfigure rollback baseline (must complete before phase 3 generates candidates)

Since this is a **reconfigure** (active seller changing published state), the rollback posture is settled **here**, before any candidate file is written:

1. Compare the live reads (`registration.registration.{revision, registrationHash}`, `registration.projection.cardHash`) against the checkpoint's recorded `artifacts.{registrationRevision, registrationHash, cardHash}`.
2. Recompute the sha256 of the checkpointed author `card.json` (the path recorded in the checkpoint's `artifacts.cardJson.path`):
   ```bash
   shasum -a 256 <card.json>   # macOS
   # or
   sha256sum <card.json>       # Linux
   ```
   Take only the first whitespace-delimited 64-hex field.
3. Compare against the checkpoint's recorded `artifacts.cardHash`.

**If all values match** (revision, registration hash, card hash, and local author card digest all agree with the checkpoint):
- Write the four live registration inputs to `<seller-repo>/registration/prev/`:
  - `storefront.json` (from `registration.registration.inputs.storefront`)
  - `rate-card.json` (from `registration.registration.inputs.rateCard`)
  - `workflow-terms.json` (from `registration.registration.inputs.workflowTerms`)
  - `card.json` (the checkpoint-verified local author card)
- Write `baseline.json` beside them:
  ```json
  {
    "sourceRevision": <live revision>,
    "sourceRegistrationHash": "<live registrationHash>",
    "sourceCardHash": "<live projection.cardHash>",
    "storefront": {"path":"registration/prev/storefront.json","sha256":"<64hex>"},
    "rateCard": {"path":"registration/prev/rate-card.json","sha256":"<64hex>"},
    "workflowTerms": {"path":"registration/prev/workflow-terms.json","sha256":"<64hex>"},
    "authorCard": {"path":"registration/prev/card.json","sha256":"<64hex>"}
  }
  ```
- Persist `rollbackBaseline.status: "verified"` in the checkpoint, with the same `source*` and `inputs` fields.

**If any proof or author input is missing or mismatched**:
- Persist `rollbackBaseline.status: "unavailable"` in the checkpoint.
- Do **not** fabricate a baseline from a platform-composed directory card.
- The owner may supply a known-good author `card.json`, or the later publish authorization must explicitly authorize roll-forward-only. Do not claim rollback exists.

**For a fresh publish** (no prior live registration): `rollbackBaseline.status: "not-applicable"`.

Persist the updated checkpoint after this step.

---

## Phase 3 — Deal shape

> Precondition: Phase 0 rollback baseline is recorded. A fresh `card fetch --pin` has succeeded since the last state change (re-pin if there's any doubt; `chain_context_complete: true` is required before validation).

### Step 3.1 — Confirm the live catalog and chosen template

Run:

```bash
kagent workflow-template list --output json --no-interactive
```

This returns the seven live templates. Confirm the seller's intended template is present in their environment.

Then read its chart to understand actual transitions and deadline edges:

```bash
kagent workflow-template get <template-id> --output json --no-interactive
```

Key fields to extract from the response:
- `chart.states.*.after.<window>.meta.seconds` for each `*Window` (deadline seconds, e.g., `deliveryWindow`, `deliveryConfirmationWindow`).
- `chart.states.REJECTED.transitions` — what can actually happen after rejection (redeliver, appeal, consent-refund, settle_mutual, or none).
- `verbs` — confirm the template supports the configuration you're about to set (e.g., `amend` exists only on `coding/v1`).

**From the checkpoint**, you should already have `seller.offer.template_id`. If it's missing or the seller is changing it, ask about deal-shape *characteristics* (never template names) using the questions in `references/template-characteristics.md`:

- "If a buyer isn't happy with what you deliver, do you want a chance to redo it, or is delivered-is-delivered?" → distinguishes redeliver-capable charts from no-redeliver charts.
- "If the buyer just goes quiet after you deliver — never confirms, never complains — should you be paid, or should they be refunded?" → splits `data-seller/v1` (silence pays seller) from `recruiting/v1` and `enriched-standard/v1` (silence refunds buyer, seller unpaid).
- "Could a delivery genuinely be disputed by a reasonable buyer, or is success obvious from the artifact itself?" → distinguishes `standard/v1` (appeal + arbitration) from the mid-lifecycle group (content-generator, coding, security-audit).
- "Is what you deliver a batch of separate items, where some can be good and others not — and would you expect to be paid for just the good ones?" → a yes points at `enriched-standard/v1`, which **v1 onboarding does not configure** (GOK-1302). Tell the seller plainly and either onboard `standard/v1` now or hand off.

If nothing fits cleanly, **propose `standard/v1`**, explain the added reject/appeal/arbitration obligations, and get explicit confirmation — don't silently derive a materially different shape.

### Step 3.2 — Generate registration candidate files

Only after the rollback baseline is confirmed in phase 0:

```bash
kagent registration template --output-dir <seller-repo>/registration/candidates --output json --no-interactive
```

This writes three skeletons that are **never overwritten** by a re-run:
- `storefront.json`
- `rate-card.json`
- `workflow-terms.json`

Each skeleton's `agentDid` must equal the publishing agent's DID (from `registration get`). Verify this before editing.

### Step 3.3 — Fill the candidates from the seller's confirmed offer

Populate each file from the checkpointed `seller.offer` and the live chart:

**`storefront.json`** — agent identity, service description, links. Use the seller's confirmed service description from `seller.offer.service_description`. Confirm the payout `status` and `address` are real, not `not-configured` or placeholder — if they're incomplete, stop and ask the owner.

**`rate-card.json`** — pricing model, currency, line items:
- **Fixed** (`fixed/v1`): `model: "fixed/v1"`, `quantity.source: "fixed"`, `quantity.value: "1"`, `negotiation.mode: "none"`, line item has `unitPriceMinor: "<price_in_minor_units>"`. No `negotiable` block.
- **Negotiated** (`negotiated/v1`): `model: "negotiated/v1"`, `escrow.basis: "negotiated"`, `negotiation.mode: "mandatory"`, line item omits `unitPriceMinor`, `negotiable[].field: "unitPriceMinor"` with `minMinor`/`maxMinor` equal to `total_min_minor`/`total_max_minor`, `totalBounds` matches the same band.
- **Reserve** (negotiated-with-reserve only): absent from the public rate card; stored in the standing orders (phase 5), not here.
- **Currency**: The `asset` field is a CAIP-19: `eip155:<chain_id>/erc20:0x<40hex>`. `chain_id` comes from the most recent successful `card fetch --pin`. **The USDC contract address has no supported CLI/env source** — this is an explicit operator handoff: ask the owner for their deployment's USDC ERC-20 address, validate the format, and stop with a precise request if they don't have it. Never invent an address.

**`workflow-terms.json`** — template ID and config overrides:

```json
{
  "schema": "urn:kiteai:passport:seller-registration:schema:workflow-terms:v0",
  "agentDid": "<did>",
  "offerings": [
    {
      "offeringId": "<offering-id>",
      "workflowTemplateId": "<template-id>",
      "config": {
        "limits": { "maxRedeliveries": <0–3, only on redeliver-capable charts> },
        "windows": { "<WindowName>": <override_seconds> },
        "parameters": {},
        "skippedStates": []
      }
    }
  ]
}
```

**Config rules:**
- A bare `{}` inherits everything — only write overrides that differ from chart defaults.
- **`maxRedeliveries`** (0–3): meaningful only on `content-generator/v1`, `coding/v1`, `security-audit/v1`. Do not configure it on templates that cannot redeliver from `REJECTED` (`standard/v1`, `enriched-standard/v1`, `data-seller/v1`, `recruiting/v1`). The catalog does not expose a pre-publish default; materialized default is `2` where applicable.
- **Window overrides**: write only windows you intend to override. Use full `*Window` names. Values are integer seconds. Never write empty members (`windows: {}`) or explicit zeros.
- **Deadline display**: merge the chart's live defaults with your overrides locally and show the seller the effective deadlines in plain language (e.g., "buyer has 2 days to confirm delivery; if they reject, you have 10 minutes to respond").

### Step 3.4 — Validate before any publish

```bash
kagent registration validate \
  --storefront <seller-repo>/registration/candidates/storefront.json \
  --rate-card <seller-repo>/registration/candidates/rate-card.json \
  --workflow-terms <seller-repo>/registration/candidates/workflow-terms.json \
  --output json --no-interactive
```

- `valid: true` → proceed to phase 4.
- Exit `8` with `details.problems[]` → read each `{input, path, message}`, fix the named artifact, re-run validation. Common fixes:
  - `eip155:0` currency → `card fetch --pin` hasn't completed or wasn't re-run after a state change. Re-pin.
  - `not-configured` payout → settlement inputs (payout `status`, `address`, USDC contract) are incomplete; stop and ask the owner.
  - Schema/ID mismatches → re-read `registration template` skeletons; don't hand-edit schema URIs.
- Exit `7` → idempotency/terms conflict; re-read and rebuild.
- **Never publish on an exit-8.** Validation is the gate.

---

## What to tell the seller now

**We are in Phase 3 (deal shape).** Phase 0 confirmed your active binding and recorded the reconfigure rollback baseline. Your checkpointed offer is the source of truth for price and description — I'll confirm it matches what you want before we proceed.

Before I generate the new registration files, I need to confirm one thing about the deal shape:

> **"If a buyer isn't happy with what you deliver, do you want a chance to redo it, or is delivered-is-delivered?"**

This determines which workflow template we configure — and whether you face appeal/arbitration obligations, or a single redeliver chance, or no dispute lane at all. (I'll also confirm what happens if the buyer goes silent after delivery — that's a separate axis that changes your risk.)

Once you answer, I'll read the live chart, generate the three registration candidate files, fill them from your confirmed offer, and validate them before anything goes live.

03 / Read the result correctly
Literal means literal. Case-sensitive substring matching, with zero-based Unicode character offsets and one-based line numbers. No regex, fuzzy matching or automatic waivers.


A pass is not a safety certificate. An agent can quote a command without executing it, mention a forbidden action, or put steps in the wrong order and still match strings. Check expected behavior and attach a transcript-bound semantic review.


Replay is not a fresh model run. Captures retain their provenance. Fixture runs test the harness; agent transcripts test responses. Neither proves skill triggering, account access or a settled payment.
